但想象一下威尼斯吧，一只峭壁鹰巢里的雌鹰，拼命拍打着双翅，试图为配偶遮挡住阳光！噢，但威尼斯的表演堪称绝妙！当然，这也多亏有了一个非常体面的借口来承载这场表演。去游泳的主意对所有人来说不啻为一场及时雨和福音。大家可以畅谈这样一个提议，而不会惹出任何麻烦。盖伊风度翩翩地把功劳归到了我的头上，我被雪莉和威尼斯盛赞为美国人所谓的那种人——威尼斯发誓指着上帝担保——一个“硬汉”。但雪莉觉得盖伊非要坚持穿泳衣，未免太扫兴了，破坏了整个野餐的兴致。雪莉长篇大论地陈述了这个观点。威尼斯也是如此。

===Original===

“I mean, on a hearty picnic like this!” said Shirley helplessly. And Venice said it was absurd to go digging about among bathing-costumes on a nice, warm, pitch-black night. One’s chemise, said Shirley, would do ever so well. One’s chemise, said Venice, had done very well before. And one wouldn’t, said Shirley, indignantly, really need the chemise afterwards, just to come home with. Not in this heat, said Venice, and they appealed to Iris, but Iris protested that she must be neutral, because she was not going to bathe; but she would have thought, she said, that a dry shift was always preferable, when possible.

“Not going to bathe!” cried Venice. “Not going to﻿—Oh, you must bathe! Of course you’re going to bathe! Oi, you’ll spoil the whole party!”

It was after dinner, and Hugo was doing a few card-tricks with champagne-glasses, the idea being, Hugo said, to settle our digestions after one of the best dinners that had ever left him with an appetite.

“Of course she’ll bathe!” said Hugo. “I’ve known the girl all my life, and I’ll answer for her. She’ll bathe. Leave her to me. Silly, not bathing.”

“She’s rather common, your friend,” Shirley sighed to Guy.

“Sickening. Cannot bathe, really, on a night like this.”

“Seems to me,” Napier scowled, “that she will have to bathe. Tell me if I’m wrong. What?”

“Listen,” Iris pleaded.

“Coming here,” said Shirley indignantly, “and not bathing!”

“I am terrified,” said Iris desperately. “Terrified of masses of water. Once, in the Black Sea of all places, I got cramp, and ever since.﻿ ﻿…”

“If you only knew,” sighed Hugo, “how cold all that leaves us! You’ll swim, girl. Good for you. Make your coat shine. Give you back your lost youth.”

“Hugo, don’t be so tactless!” cried Shirley.

“The girl’s right,” Guy closed the discussion. “She’s only been out of bed about a month.﻿ ﻿…”

“But I haven’t been near mine for longer than that!” cried Shirley inevitably, and it was just at that moment, under cover of it, that I touched the ice-cold hand. That was the only sign until we reached the river that Venice’s married life had tumbled like a house of cards about her heart, that and her “trusting” Iris.

===Chinese===

“我是说，在这么尽兴的野餐上！”雪莉无可奈何地说。威尼斯也附和道，在一个温暖怡人、漆黑一团的夜晚还要到处翻找泳衣简直荒唐透顶。雪莉说，套一件衬裙就足够应付了。威尼斯说，自己的衬裙以前就应付得很好。而且雪莉义愤填膺地说，游完之后其实根本不需要再穿衬裙，光着身子直接回家就行。在这种天气下，威尼斯说，随后她们求助于艾丽丝，但艾丽丝声明自己必须保持中立，因为她不去游泳；不过她说，她一直觉得只要条件允许，一件干爽的衬裙总是更好的选择。

“不去游泳！”威尼斯喊道，“不去——噢，你必须游！你当然得去游！喂，你会把整个聚会都搞砸的！”

那是晚餐之后，雨果正在拿香槟酒杯表演纸牌戏法，按雨果的说法，这有助于消化刚刚吃完的平生最棒的一顿大餐。

“她当然会游！”雨果说，“我和这姑娘从小一起长大，我替她打包票。她会游的。交给我吧。不游泳太傻了。”

“你那位朋友可真粗俗，”雪莉冲着盖伊叹气道。

“真叫人恶心。在这样的夜晚，确实不能不下水。”

“依我看，”内皮尔皱起眉头，“她是非游不可了。如果我说错了就纠正我。嗯？”

“听我说，”艾丽丝恳求道。

“跑到这里来，”雪莉愤愤不平地说，“竟然不游泳！”

“我吓坏了，”艾丽丝绝望地说，“我害怕大片的水域。有一次，偏偏在黑海，我抽筋了，从那以后。……”

“你要是知道，”雨果叹息道，“这话听在我们耳朵里有多冷淡就好了！你会游的，姑娘。对你有好处。能让你的毛发光亮。能让你找回失去的青春。”

“雨果，别这么不识趣！”雪莉喊道。

“姑娘说得对，”盖伊结束了争论，“她大病初愈下床才不过一个月左右。……”

“可我都离开自己的床不止一个月了！”雪莉不由自主地嚷道，正是借着这句话的掩护，我碰到了那只冰凉如铁的手。直到我们抵达河畔之前，那是威尼斯的婚姻生活如纸牌屋般在她心中轰然崩塌的唯一迹象，除了那一句她“信任”艾丽丝之外。

===Original===

Venice was saying: “And didn’t we just have some trouble with you, Mrs. Storm, when you were ill in Paris! Naps white in the face thinking you were going to die, me green in the face thinking my holiday would be spoilt if you did, this he-man here purple in the face telling me to be reasonable.﻿ ﻿…”

“But you were in bed for ages, weren’t you?” said Shirley sympathetically. “What was it? Some foul plague?”

“Ptomaine poisoning,” said Napier, and as I was giving Venice a light with which to torture yet another cigarette my hand happened to touch hers. “In this heat!” said I.

“Shut up, you fool!” she whispered desperately, and then she tried not to smile frantically, whispering: “Darling, darling, *darling*! My one friend.﻿ ﻿…”

“Venice, they’re never any real good, friends. They can’t *do* anything.﻿ ﻿…”

“I know. Oh, I know. Oh God, I know!﻿ ﻿… Mrs. Storm, what a divine lipstick! May I see? May I use?”

Baby.

===Chinese===

威尼斯正在说道：“而且当你在巴黎生病时，斯托姆夫人，我们可真为你操碎了心！纳普斯脸色发白以为你要死了，我脸色发青心想你要是死了我的假期就泡汤了，而这位硬汉脸色发紫叫我理智一点。……”

“可你躺在床上躺了很久，对吧？”雪莉关切地问，“到底是什么病？某种可怕的瘟疫吗？”

“尸碱中毒，”内皮尔说，当我递给威尼斯一个火点着去折磨又一支香烟时，我的手碰巧碰到了她的手。“在这种天气下！”我说。

“闭嘴，你这个笨蛋！”她绝望地耳语道，随后她努力克制着不露出疯狂的笑容，低语道：“亲爱的，亲爱的，亲爱的！我唯一的朋友。……”

“威尼斯，朋友向来起不到任何实际作用。他们什么都做不了。……”

“我知道。噢，我知道。上帝啊，我知道！……斯托姆夫人，多么神圣的口红啊！我能看看吗？我能用用吗？”

小孩子。

### II / 第二节
===Original===

Thus, the children’s party.﻿ ﻿…

Their engines no louder than a whisper through the quiet noises of the night, and swift as arrows with flaming eyes, two touring-cars, a primrose and a blue, passed through the villages riverwards. The good people slept on undisturbed, as why should they not, for a motorcar will disturb the amenities of a village by night less than a wheelbarrow. Maybe through the crack of a blind flashed a startling light on a sleepless pillow. Maybe a distant scream, as of a great seabird, stirred a boy to dream of vain, polite, perishable delights. Maybe a cow stared thoughtfully at the strange, swift, whirring insects with the livid eyes and the cruel screams. Here and there the lamps shone on the buttons of a policeman, stock-still in a doorway. There was no air but the wind of our passage, warm, heavy with dust and dry grasses. “Rain, rain!” breathed England in her sleep. And there was no rain, nor breath of rain, nor yet that damp, oppressive foretaste of a thunderstorm to come, only a torment of heat over the land and around the land the unclouded darkness pinned with faint stars. A myriad flies withstood the stork, were appalled, died. Wrapped in silence, armed with light, we fled beneath the suns of the night like battle-chariots rushing to the assault of the stronghold of the gods. Iris had gone mad.

I thought of Mr. Polly disturbed in his sleep, twenty years ago, on a Sussex hayrick by the roar of a racing-car. Mr. Polly could have slept undisturbed for us. One hundred and twenty horses drew us, shadows of nothing from nothing to nothing beneath the impersonal stare of the stars. Look away from the stars, lovers of the world’s delights, for they are the destroyers of the world’s delights with their dreams of grander things. To listen to great music, to adore God in vast solitudes, to kneel before the face of beauty, to pass through the quiet land like an arrow with flaming eyes, swifter than your thoughts: such and the like, according to each our nature, are the captains of the world’s delights, so keep your eyes from the stars, that destroy our delights with their dreams of grander things.

===Chinese===

就这样，孩子们的聚会开始了。……

两辆旅行敞篷车，一辆报春花黄，一辆蓝色，引擎声在寂静的夜色中轻微得犹如耳语，疾驰如带着灼热双眼的利箭，穿过村庄奔向河畔。善良的村民们安然沉睡未受惊扰，他们理应如此，因为一辆机动车在夜间对乡村宁静的打扰甚至还不如一辆独轮手推车。也许透过百叶窗的缝隙，一道刺眼的光芒闪现在不眠的枕头上。也许远处一声啼鸣，如同一只巨大的海鸟，勾起少年对虚幻、优雅、易逝欢愉的梦境。也许一头母牛若有所思地凝视着带着惨白双眸和残酷尖啸的奇特而迅疾的飞虫。路灯不时照在伫立在门廊前纹丝不动的警察制服扣子上。除了一路疾驰掀起的狂风，没有任何空气流动，风是温热的，夹杂着尘土和干草的沉重气息。“下雨吧，下雨吧！”英国在睡梦中喘息着。然而没有雨，没有一丝雨的气息，甚至连雷雨将至时那种潮湿压抑的预兆都没有，只有笼罩在国土上的酷热煎熬，以及四周缀着微弱繁星的澄澈夜空。无数飞虫迎向车头的鹳鸟车标，惊骇万状，粉身碎骨。我们被寂静包裹，以强光为武器，在夜空的群星下飞驰，宛如冲锋陷阵去攻打众神要塞的战车。艾丽丝疯了。

我想起了二十年前在萨塞克斯的一个干草堆上被赛车轰鸣惊醒的波利先生。波利先生遇到我们本可以安然酣睡。一百二十匹马力牵引着我们，在群星漠然的凝视下，从虚无驶向虚无的虚无阴影。避开星辰的视线吧，尘世欢愉的追逐者们，因为星辰正以它们更宏伟的梦想摧毁着尘世的欢愉。倾听宏伟的乐章，在辽阔的孤独中敬拜上帝，在美丽的容颜前屈膝，如一支长着烈火双眼的利箭飞越静谧的大地，比思绪还要疾驰：凡此种种，因人而异，都是尘世欢愉的统帅，所以别去看群星，因为群星正以更宏伟的梦想摧毁着我们的欢愉。

===Original===

Silence marches with the thoughts in your mind. Maybe a word or two will drop, hesitate in the wind, fight with the dying hosts of midgets, perish on the road. Small flying things brush by your face, and a dry unsweet scent, as though England is sleeping with her windows closed.

The green hat was somewhere beside me, it fell and rolled about my feet, she murmured: “Leave it.” To the warm wind fell the honour of the dance, and with the tawny cornstalks the wind stepped a wide-flung dance. Why does your hair dance so, Iris March, like a halo possessed of devils? Why this, why that, Iris March?

In the glass of the windscreen we might now and then see the faint reflection of Guy’s lamps behind us. Nay, once or twice his bonnet nosed up beside Iris, just beside her elbow. But the stork cried hoarsely, flew on.

Again, silent as the rustle of a woman’s dress walking in a dark garden, Guy’s shining bonnet menaced the tail of our eye, and Guy himself, alone in front, yellow-haired, grim, fair herald of a fighting pageant in his brilliant Fair Isle sweater, and now the face of Venice, leaning forward to Guy’s shoulder, excited, exhorting. Venice, for Venice! She would pass the lady of the dancing hair, would Venice. But the stork cried hoarsely, flew on.

We wrestled. Silent as phantoms, we wrestled. One hundred and twenty horses, a winged Mercury and a stork wrestled for the dominion of nothing on the Reading road.

There was a corner, proud and saturnine from many fell triumphs. The stork screamed a taunt, flew on.

“Ho!” gasped Hugo, chattering, from behind. “Steady, girl! Shirley’s frightened.﻿ ﻿…”

“Let him pass, Iris!” cried I. A little scared, a woman driving, you never know, might lose her head, boy’s head, curly head, white and tiger-tawny, but too white, too intent, too infernally reckless.﻿ ﻿…

“Iris, Iris!”

“Can do seventy-five, if you like,” cried the lips of the dancing hair.

“Let him pass, Iris!”

“Pass? Am I mad! As soon let happiness pass! See, the stars are laughing.﻿ ﻿…”

“Iris, Iris!”

“Let him pass, Iris! Damn you, it won’t hold the road!”

“Why, the road’s fainting with joy! Can do seventy-six if you like. But not more.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

沉默与你脑海中的思绪一同行进。也许有一两句话会飘落，在风中犹疑，与成群垂死的飞虫搏斗，消逝在公路之上。细小的飞行生物拂过你的面庞，空气中弥漫着一股干涩不甜的气味，仿佛英国正紧闭着窗户在沉睡。

绿礼帽就在我身旁某处，它掉落下来，在我的脚边翻滚，她低声说：“别管它。”跳舞的荣誉落到了温暖的风中，伴随着黄褐色的麦秸，风踏出了一曲大开大合的舞蹈。你的头发为何这般飞舞，艾丽丝·马奇，宛如魔鬼附身的光环？为何这样，为何那样，艾丽丝·马奇？

在挡风玻璃的倒影中，我们偶尔能看到身后盖伊车灯的微弱反光。不仅如此，有一两次他的车头甚至贴到了艾丽丝的身侧，就在她的手肘旁。然而鹳鸟沙哑地尖叫着，继续飞翔。

紧接着，如同女人在幽暗花园中漫步时裙摆窸窣的声响般悄无声息，盖伊闪闪发光的车头再次威胁着我们的眼角余光；盖伊独自一人坐在前面，金发、冷峻，身穿色彩斑斓的费尔岛毛衣，犹如一场征战盛典上的英俊传令官；而此刻威尼斯的脸正探向前靠在盖伊的肩膀上，兴奋地鼓励着。威尼斯，为了威尼斯！她要超过那个长着飞舞秀发的女士，威尼斯誓要如此。然而鹳鸟沙哑地尖叫着，继续飞翔。

我们搏斗着。如同幽灵般寂静，我们搏斗着。一百二十匹马力，一个长着翅膀的墨丘利与一只鹳鸟，在雷丁公路上为争夺虚无的统治权而搏斗。

前方有一处弯道，因多次残酷的险胜而显得高傲阴郁。鹳鸟发出一声嘲弄的尖叫，疾飞而过。

“呼！”雨果在后座牙齿打战地倒抽了一口凉气，“稳住，姑娘！雪莉吓坏了。……”

“让她超过去，艾丽丝！”我喊道。我有点害怕，女人开车，你永远说不准，她可能会失去理智，男孩子的头颅，卷曲的头颅，雪白而如虎般黄褐，但太白了，太专注了，太该死地不顾一切了。……

“艾丽丝，艾丽丝！”

“只要你愿意，能开到七十五英里，”跳动头发下的双唇喊道。

“让她过去，艾丽丝！”

“过去？我疯了吗！不如让幸福擦肩而过！看哪，星星都在笑呢。……”

“艾丽丝，艾丽丝！”

“让她过去，艾丽丝！见鬼，车要抓不住地面了！”

“哎，公路高兴得都要晕过去了！只要你乐意，能开到七十六英里。但不能再多了。……”

===Original===

A new road, recently laid down to soften the passage of footlight-favourites to the reaches of Taplow and Maidenhead, wide, deserted of houses. Meadows swept each side into the desert darkness. Iris, perhaps remembering Mr. Polly, perhaps thinking Mr. Polly had slept long enough, kicked open the exhaust. That lends another mile an hour to speed. Another sixty horses gave answer behind, then fell snarling back towards London. “Seventy-one, Iris!”

“Ow!” she breathed. “Accelerator burning foot. Ow! Hell!”

“Maidenhead!” screamed Shirley.

“To the right, Iris!”

And so we came into the yard of Quindle’s. Still, sleeping, shuttered, Quindle’s hostelry was a rebuke to the flaming lights which made a festival of the desert scene. Then Guy’s car swung in, poor winged Mercury. Shows one, don’t you know, how much gods are worth.﻿ ﻿…

“Sickening, Iris. You had me properly beat that time.”

“But how my foot burns, Guy!”

“Look!” said Venice. “Hist!”

A man in shirtsleeves was come out of the hotel. He stared at us, rubbed his eyes, stared at us.

“Ho!” called Major Cypress. “Ho, there! Is that Quindle’s speaking?”

The man in shirtsleeves came through the flame of the lamps. An amiable man, he looked.

“Now remember,” whispered Shirley at large, “no matter how beastly they are to us, we are going to bathe. Let everyone speak at once. That will baffle him.”

“Evening,” said the man in shirtsleeves. “Bit late, isn’t it?”

“Not one yet,” said Hugo. “I say, we want to bathe.”

“Can’t have no rooms,” said the man in shirtsleeves. “Hotel’s full.”

“But we don’t want no rooms!” Venice pleaded. “We only want to bathe.﻿ ﻿…”

“Bar’s closed,” said the man in shirtsleeves.

“Serve you right,” said Hugo. “But we’ll give you a drink if you want one. Here you are. Beer or champagne?”

“I want to bathe,” Shirley pleaded.

“Can’t bathe ’ere,” said the man in shirtsleeves.

“You don’t know about us,” said Venice severely. “We can bath anywhere.”

“Against the lor, miss.”

“That will be all right about the law.” A sudden voice, a calm voice, a cold, chill murmur. It fell from heights like a douche. The man in shirtsleeves tried not to have to look up all the way to Guy’s face. Too tall was Guy, in that light. Guy smiled down at the man in shirtsleeves.

“Hot night,” Guy murmured. “Very hot. My children, all these.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

一条新修的公路，最近刚铺好，以方便舞台名角们前往塔普洛和梅登黑德的河段，路面宽阔，两旁没有房屋。草甸在两侧向荒凉的黑暗中铺展。艾丽丝也许是想起了波利先生，也许是觉得波利先生已经睡得够久了，一脚踩开了排气门。这能让车速每小时再增加一英里。身后另有六十匹马力做出了呼应，随后咆哮着退向伦敦方向。“七十一英里了，艾丽丝！”

“哎哟！”她喘息道，“油门把脚烫得要命。哎哟！真见鬼！”

“梅登黑德到啦！”雪莉尖叫起来。

“向右拐，艾丽丝！”

就这样，我们开进了昆德尔客栈的院子。寂静、沉睡、门窗紧闭，昆德尔客栈宛如对这片荒凉景象中如节日般耀眼的车灯的一记无声谴责。接着盖伊的车也拐了进来，可怜的带翅墨丘利。这让人明白，神明到底值几分钱。……

“真叫人恶心，艾丽丝。这次你可把我彻底比下去了。”

“可是我的脚烫得有多厉害啊，盖伊！”

“看！”威尼斯说，“嘘！”

一个穿着衬衫袖子的男人从客栈里走了出来。他盯着我们，揉了揉眼睛，又盯着我们。

“喂！”塞普里斯少校喊道，“喂，那边的！是昆德尔客栈在说话吗？”

穿衬衫袖子的男人穿过大灯的光柱走过来。他看起来像个和善的人。

“现在记住，”雪莉冲着大家耳语道，“不管他们对我们态度多恶劣，我们都要游泳。大家一起开口。这样能把他搞晕。”

“晚上好，”穿衬衫袖子的男人说，“有点太晚了吧？”

“还没到一点呢，”雨果说，“我说，我们要游泳。”

“没有空房间了，”穿衬衫袖子的男人说，“客栈客满了。”

“可我们不要房间！”威尼斯央求道，“我们只想游泳。……”

“酒吧关门了，”穿衬衫袖子的男人说。

“算你们活该，”雨果说，“但如果你想喝一杯，我们可以请你。给你。啤酒还是香槟？”

“我想游泳，”雪莉恳求道。

“这儿不能游泳，”穿衬衫袖子的男人说。

“你还不了解我们，”威尼斯严肃地说，“我们在任何地方都能游泳。”

“这违反法律，小姐。”

“法律的事包在我们身上。”一个突如其来的声音，平静、冰冷、清脆的低语。如同一道冷水从高处浇下。穿衬衫袖子的男人极力避免一路仰头去看盖伊的脸。在那道光线下，盖伊实在太高了。盖伊低头俯视着穿衬衫袖子的男人，微笑着。

“天气真热，”盖伊低语道，“太热了。这些全都是我的孩子们。……”

===Original===

“Ho,” said the man in shirtsleeves. “ ’Ot or cold, it’s against the lor, that’s wot.”

“Don’t you worry your head about the law,” said Guy. “But what you might do, now, would be to get us some towels. We forgot towels.﻿ ﻿…”

“Against the lor, anyhow,” said the man in shirtsleeves.

“I do wish you’d say something else just once,” snapped Shirley.

Iris, a white face, gardenia-white, mocking hair, a barbaric scarf about her throat, her hat a splash of black against the frail fancy that was her dress, standing a little away, staring at the stars. “A light,” she murmured. “A light!” Then Napier was beside her, lighting her cigarette, lighting also the curious, still smile on his acolyte’s face, an enchanted smile, the smile of a man drowned in a magic pool. The collar of his white shirt was unbuttoned, the dark hair sleek in the glow of the flaming lights.﻿ ﻿…

“Naps, give me a light, too.”

“Here you are, Venice,” said Hugo.

“Oh, it’s gone out! Naps, a light!”

“Sorry, Venice.﻿ ﻿…”

Guy seemed to be shaking hands with the man in shirtsleeves.

“Get you some towels,” said he, moving off.

Hugo whispered: “One law for the rich, one for the poor. Dear me!”

“Sickening. But we may as well take advantage of what’s left of it. Getting a bit mouldy, that law.”

“Come on, Hugo. God, it’s dark! Which way is it? We must find a boat.﻿ ﻿…”

“Naps, this way! No, down here﻿ ﻿… but hang on to my arm! Soon find a boat.﻿ ﻿…”

“You can’t have no boat!” called the man in shirtsleeves.

“You get those towels,” said Hugo severely. “The way you talk!”

“Please, your arm,” Iris begged me, husky voice. “Foot hurts. And isn’t it dark!”

“Here you are!” came Venice’s clear boy’s voice from the pit of darkness ahead, beneath us.

We faltered, blind as bats, down the slope of a landing-stage.

“Matches, please!” Shirley’s voice. Oh, trust Shirley and Venice to have the affair well in hand! The pit of darkness ahead was bitten by tiny flames. “Oh, look out, Naps! Ow, God damn you!”

“Naps, you might wipe your feet on your own wife, would you?”

There were uncertainties, holes, fissures of splintered wood. The tiny flames in the pit ahead were like lance-points thrusting the darkness deeper into the eyes.

===Chinese===

“哼，”穿衬衫袖子的男人说，“热也好冷也好，反正违反法律，就是这么回事。”

“你用不着为法律操心，”盖伊说，“但你现在能做的，是去帮我们拿几条毛巾。我们忘了带毛巾。……”

“不管怎样，就是违反法律，”穿衬衫袖子的男人说。

“我真希望你能哪怕一次说点别的，”雪莉厉声反驳道。

艾丽丝，一张苍白的面孔，如栀子花般雪白，带着嘲弄般的头发，脖子上围着一条充满异域野性色彩的围巾，她的帽子在她身上那件薄如蝉翼的华服映衬下宛如一抹黑色墨痕，她站在稍远一点的地方，凝视着繁星。“借个火，”她低语道，“借个火！”内皮尔便立刻来到了她身旁，为她点着香烟，也照亮了他侍僧般面庞上那奇特、静止的微笑——一个着了魔的微笑，一个溺死在魔法池水中的男人的微笑。他白衬衫的衣领敞开着，在烈火般的光芒照耀下，黑发油亮整洁。……

“纳普斯，也给我借个火。”

“给你，威尼斯，”雨果说。

“噢，灭了！纳普斯，借个火！”

“对不起，威尼斯。……”

盖伊似乎正在和穿衬衫袖子的男人握手。

“这就去给你们拿几条毛巾，”那人说着走开了。

雨果低声耳语：“对富人一套法律，对穷人另一套法律。天哪！”

“真叫人恶心。但我们不妨好好利用一下它剩下的残渣。那条法律已经有点发霉变质了。”

“来吧，雨果。天哪，太黑了！往哪边走？我们必须找条船。……”

“纳普斯，走这边！不，从这儿下去……抓紧我的胳膊！马上就能找到船。……”

“你们不能乘船！”穿衬衫袖子的男人喊道。

“你拿你的毛巾去吧，”雨果严厉地说，“看你那废话多的！”

“求求你，扶我一把，”艾丽丝沙哑地恳求我，“脚疼。而且这儿真黑啊！”

“在这儿呢！”从我们前方下方的幽暗深坑里，传来了威尼斯清脆若男孩般的声音。

我们像蝙蝠一样瞎撞着，踉踉跄跄地顺着码头的斜坡走下去。

“火柴，拜托！”雪莉的声音。噢，相信雪莉和威尼斯绝对能掌控大局！前方的漆黑深坑被微小的火苗撕咬开来。“噢，小心点，纳普斯！哎哟，该死的！”

“纳普斯，你大可以在你自己的老婆身上擦脚，行吗？”

周围是一片混乱。

===Original===

Iris and I marched slowly as the smoke of our cigarettes in the breathless night. She leaned on my arm, completely. “Foot hurts.” I wished she wouldn’t. I almost said, “don’t.” Her touch confounded, confused. She was tangible, until she touched you. She was finite, until she touched you. She was a woman, until she touched you. Then she became woman, and you water. She became a breath of womanhood clothed in the soft, delicious mystery of the flesh. Touching her, you touched all desire. She was impersonal and infinite, like all desire. She was indifferent to all but her desire, like all desire. She was a breath carved in flesh, like all desire. She was the flower of the plant of all desire. Desire is the name of the plant that Lilith sowed, and every now and then it puts out the flower that in the choir of flowers is the paramour of the mandrake.

“You are very silent, Iris.﻿ ﻿…”

“Yes﻿ ﻿… yes? Sometimes.﻿ ﻿… I don’t know, but it’s as though the stars make me nervous, sometimes. They’re so hopeless. They sneer, I can’t help thinking. But are we going right?”

The darkness ahead stirred with tiny flames and exultant voices. Venice and Shirley!

“I say, lovely boat!” cried Shirley.

“Where, Shirley?” I called.

“Between you and me,” Iris whispered, “I wouldn’t mind sitting. Foot hurts.﻿ ﻿…”

“Come straight on. Don’t go right or left. River.”

“It’s not a boat at all!” cried Venice. “It’s a lovely motor-canoe. Oh, chaps!”

“Ssh!” Guy’s voice.

Who cared? Not Shirley. “And cushions! And steering-wheel! And everything.﻿ ﻿…”

“Naps, this way! Here you are! Isn’t it a beauty?”

“Hope no one gets drowned,” Iris whispered.

“Everyone’s cold sober.”

“But weeds and cramps and things.﻿ ﻿…”

“And currents,” came Guy’s murmur from somewhere just above our heads. “But it’s safe as houses as long as we keep in a line between this bank and the other. Had inquiries made today.”

“Sensible Guy!”

“Best way to mend things is to stop them, Iris.”

Our eyes pricked by the wicked little match-lights, we could just make out at our feet the shape of a long motor-canoe and, at one end of it, a jumble of figures. They seemed to be fighting, those figures, bent this way and that in heroic attitudes. The canoe twisted and rocked frantically on its moorings. Fierce whispers, wicked words.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

艾丽丝和我走得很慢，就像在这窒息闷热的黑夜里我们嘴中香烟飘散的烟雾。她完全依靠在我的手臂上。“脚疼。”我真希望她别这样。我差点脱口说出“别碰我”。她的触碰让人困惑、心烦意乱。在碰你之前，她是清晰可触的。在碰你之前，她是有限有形的。她是一个女人，甚至是一个轻浮的女人，她是你朋友的朋友，一个穿着薄裙的漂亮女人。但在她的触碰下，在黑夜里她轻柔的依靠下，艾丽丝·马奇仿佛成了某种令人战栗的精魂，一个在暗夜里流浪的受难灵魂。

我们来到了一处宽阔木板码头的边缘。一艘长长的独木舟在水面上轻轻摇晃。

===Original===

“Steady a moment,” said Guy, just beside us. But they weren’t steady any moments, Venice and Shirley and Hugo, whilst Napier helped them by getting in their way. They were up to something, those frantic figures.

“Steady, I said!” said Guy sharply. That learnt them. Someone in the boat lit a match, and the water shone like black silk. I saw Napier’s white face looking towards us, white face, dark eyes. Love-lost, dreaming.﻿ ﻿…

“Now look here,” said Guy gently. “Just leave those sickening ropes alone. You, Venice, you!”

“But, Guy! We must get﻿—”

“Must nothing. It’s not our boat, Venice, and I never break more than one law a night.”

“But﻿—Oh, damn the man!”

“Honest to God, Venice. Now, Shirley, behave yourself! We’ll sit in the wretched boat, but no more. And the river just here is safe.﻿ ﻿…”

“Look here,” said Hugo. “What about this for an idea? The women have one end of the boat and we the other?”

“And no matches to﻿—”

“But where’s Iris?”

“Here,” came her voice, as though from the water. “In the middle of the boat. Very comfortable. Many cushions. I’ll take care of the boat while you swim.﻿ ﻿…”

“Isn’t she kind, our fast friend! I say, no matches to be struck until someone gives the word! My figure’s good, but even Reville doesn’t think it’s perfect.﻿ ﻿…”

“And better hand all your nasty bits of jewellery and watches to Iris.”

“ ’Ere’s towels,” said a miserable voice.

The canoe rocked beneath us. At our end soft things dropped to our feet, got in the way. Never was so dark and still a night. It was a relief taking off even the white flannels.

“Any swimming to be done,” said Guy, “to be done in a straight line between this and the other bank. First man or woman who disobeys gets a crack on the head (a) from the bridge over there and (b) from me.”

“This wing’s getting a bit crowded,” sighed Hugo. “It’s a blessing we’re not French and haven’t nice warm underclothes as well.”

The glow of her cigarette lit Iris’s mouth and eyes.﻿ ﻿…

“I got one foot in the water,” she said at large.

“Taking the edge off our bathing!” Dear Hugo.﻿ ﻿…

“Now, wot’s all this about bathing?” said a Voice.

“Police! Puss, puss!”

“Didn’t I tell ’em!” panted the man in shirtsleeves. “Didn’t I! Told ’em it was against the lor.”

“Look here!” cried Venice from the pit of darkness. “Don’t you put that bull’s-eye this way, else God knows what you won’t see!”

===Chinese===

“稳住一会儿，”盖伊就在我们身旁说道。但威尼斯、雪莉和雨果片刻也稳不住，而内皮尔挡在他们路上更是帮了倒忙。那些狂乱的身影正忙活着什么。

“我说了，稳住！”盖伊厉声喝道。这下教训了他们。船里有人划了一根火柴，水面闪烁如黑丝绸。我看见内皮尔苍白的面孔正朝我们望来，苍白的面孔，漆黑的双眸。为爱迷失，如在梦中。……

“听我说，”盖伊温和地说，“别去碰那些该死的缆绳。特别是你，威尼斯，你！”

“可是，盖伊！我们必须——”

“没什么必须不必须的。这不是我们的船，威尼斯，我一晚上从来不打破一条以上的法律。”

“但是——噢，见鬼的男人！”

“天地良心，威尼斯。好啦，雪莉，规矩点！我们坐在倒霉的船里，但仅此而已。而且这儿附近的河段很安全。……”

“听我说，”雨果说，“这个主意怎么样？女人们待在船的一头，我们待在另一头？”

“而且不准划火柴——”

“可是艾丽丝呢？”

“在这儿呢，”传来她的声音，仿佛来自水面，“在船中间。很舒服。好多靠垫。你们游泳的时候我来照看船。……”

“她多体贴啊，我们这位随和的朋友！我说，谁也不许划火柴，除非有人发话！我的身材是不错，但就连雷维尔都不认为它是完美的。……”

“而且最好把你们那些讨厌的首饰和手表全交给艾丽丝保管。”

“毛巾来啦，”一个凄惨的声音说。

独木舟在我们身下摇晃。在我们这一头，柔软的衣物掉落到了我们的脚边，绊手绊脚。从来没有过如此黑暗寂静的夜晚。哪怕只是脱下白色法兰绒裤也是一种解脱。

“要是有人游泳，”盖伊说，“必须在这边岸和对岸之间游直线。第一个违规的男女，脑袋上都会挨一记闷棍：(a) 挨那边的桥砸；(b) 挨我砸。”

“这一侧越来越挤了，”雨果叹息道，“谢天谢地我们不是法国人，没有穿一身暖和的内衣。”

艾丽丝香烟的微光照亮了她的双唇与眼眸。……

“我有一只脚伸进水里了，”她随口说道。

“这简直是在挫伤我们游泳的积极性！”亲爱的雨果。……

“喂，游泳是怎么回事？”一个声音响起。

“警察来啦！咪咪，咪咪！”

“我不是早跟他们说了嘛！”穿衬衫袖子的男人气喘吁吁地说，“我说过了吧！我告诉过他们这违反法律。”

“听着！”威尼斯从黑暗的深坑里喊道，“别把手电筒往这边照，否则天晓得你会看到什么！”

===Original===

“And he’ll never go back to his wife again,” sang Shirley. “I know men.”

“You ain’t allowed to swim here,” said the Voice tremendously. “Are they, Bill? Out of it, now!”

“I do wish,” Hugo said violently, “that perfect strangers wouldn’t force themselves on us like this. Anyone would think we were at a Royal Garden Party!”

The canoe rocked frantically. “Damn you, Guy!” said Napier. The constable turned his bull’s-eye to where he thought Guy’s face would be, then flashed it a foot higher.

“About this law,” murmured Guy.

“Now, sir,” said the Voice, rather pathetically I thought. “I don’t want to have no trouble.”

“The very word! I was just going to ask you if it would be troubling you too much to ask you to run up to your house to lend us some towels. It really would be very kind of you. Our friend here hasn’t brought us quite enough.﻿ ﻿…”

Splash!

“Look ’ere,” began the Voice desperately.

“Don’t look, constable! Be strong. Use your willpower. Women are but idle vanities.”

“Oo!” gurgled Venice. “If you only knew how lovely it was! Come on, everybody. Oh, it’s so warm!”

“Now remember, Venice﻿—in a straight line between the banks.”

“That’s right, sir,” said the Voice.

“You and your banks!” sighed Shirley. Splash! “Ow, it’s freezing!”

“An’ it’s not their boat!” pleaded the man in shirtsleeves. “They got no right in that boat. It’s Lord Lamorna’s, that is.”

“Good Lord, Johnny’s! And he’s kept it hidden from us!” Splash! “Where are you, Venice? Shirley?”

“Napier, be careful!” cried Iris, laughed Iris.﻿ ﻿…

“Are you gentlemen saying as you’re friends of Lord Lamorna’s?” asked the voice.

“Friends!” said Hugo. “I won’t know him. We served together in Romano’s Riflemen, but now he’ll be jolly lucky if we don’t scuttle his boat. Owes me a fiver. Goodbye.”

The river was warm, soft, quiet. Most un-English were the waters of the Thames that night, most Italianate. Never before had one understood the verity of that phrase “on the bosom of the waters.”

From several yards away I could see the long shape of the motor-canoe. How Lamorna’s creditors would like to hear of that canoe! Hugo would blackmail him for his fiver. Dear Hugo. Suddenly the glow of Iris’s cigarette stabbed the darkness, and maybe that was her shadow there, and that the one foot in the water.﻿ ﻿…

“Who’s that?” she gasped.

===Chinese===

“他再也不会回到妻子身边了，”雪莉唱道，“我了解男人。”

“这里不准游泳，”那个声音威严地响起，“能游吗，比尔？快出来，现在就出来！”

“我真希望，”雨果粗暴地说，“完全陌生的人不要像这样硬往我们身边凑。换成谁都会以为我们是在参加皇家花园派对呢！”

独木舟剧烈摇晃。“该死的，盖伊！”内皮尔说。巡警把他手电筒的光斑移向他以为盖伊脸庞所在的位置，随后又不得不往上移了一英尺。

“关于这条法律嘛，”盖伊低语道。

“听着，先生，”那个声音说，我觉得语气挺可怜的，“我可不想惹什么麻烦。”

“正是这话！我正想问您，要是麻烦您跑回您家借给我们几条毛巾，会不会太麻烦您了？那您可真是太好心了。我们这位朋友带来的毛巾不太够用。……”

扑通！

“听着，”那个声音绝望地开头道。

“别看，警官！坚强点。发挥你的意志力。女人不过是虚妄的浮华。”

“哎哟！”威尼斯咯咯笑道，“你要是知道这有多舒服就好了！快来呀，大家。噢，水好暖和啊！”

“记住，威尼斯——在两岸之间游直线。”

“这就对了，先生，”那个声音说。

“你和你的两岸！”雪莉叹息道。扑通！“哎哟，冻死人啦！”

“而且这不是他们的船！”穿衬衫袖子的男人恳求道，“他们没权利上那条船。那是拉莫纳勋爵的船，就是他的。”

“天哪，约翰尼的！他竟然一直瞒着我们！”扑通！“你在哪儿，威尼斯？雪莉？”

“内皮尔，小心点！”艾丽丝喊道，艾丽丝笑了起来。……

“几位先生是说，你们是拉莫纳勋爵的朋友？”那个声音问。

“朋友！”雨果说，“我才不认识他呢。我们当年一起在罗马诺步枪队服役，但现在要是我们不把他的船凿沉，他就谢天谢地了。他还欠我五镑钱呢。再见。”

河水温暖、轻柔、静谧。那个夜晚泰晤士河的水流一点都不像英国的河水，倒极具意大利风情。人从来不曾如此真切地理解过“在水之波心”这句话的真谛。

从几码外，我能看见那条机动独木舟长长的轮廓。拉莫纳的债主们要是听说这艘独木舟该有多高兴啊！雨果会为了那五镑钱敲诈他。亲爱的雨果。突然，艾丽丝香烟的微光刺破了黑暗，也许那是她在上面的影子，那是她探入水中的一只脚。……

“是谁？”她倒吸了一口凉气。

===Original===

I was anchored to her ankle. My hand could have gone twice round it.

“Take care of them,” she whispered. “Dear, take care of them. Keep your eyes on that Venice child. She’s reckless. Quick, and catch them up. I rely on you somehow﻿—”

“You mustn’t, Iris. I am enemy to Iris Storm.”

“Oh, friends and enemies! One relies on what people are in themselves, no matter what circumstances may make them feel.”

“And circumstances, Iris﻿—do they make a woman so heartless?”

“Heartless! That’s a large word, rather. Heartless? But maybe I am tired of being unhappy. So maybe I walked into a garden and built a high wall round it. Oh, may be, may be! Dear friend, go after them now. I am nervous, they’re so young. By their voices, they seem to have gone very far.﻿ ﻿…”

But from the water the voices seemed to come from within a foot of one’s ear. They must, I thought, be straight ahead, towards the opposite bank. Swiftly a whisper cut the water near me, past me. “Young slacker!” came Guy’s murmur. But I, not for exercise was I on the bosom of the waters that night. I lazed, listening to the voices ahead, sharp and clear across the water. Dimly, softly, clammy-cold, a weed would brush one. The stars were like the lance-points of a mighty host marching down to the chastisement of the world. But the darkness baffled them, whilst I floated into the heart of it, I loitered.

“Mind your head on this quay here, Venice! Venice! Hello, where’s Venice?”

“Here. I say, what’s this place?”

“Oh, my pretty dears, why isn’t one always in the water! I say, what’s this wooden thing?”

“Looks like a landing-stage to me. What? I say, Hugo, what’s this place? What?”

“Am I a graduate of Maidenhead, asking me? But let’s try the place, anyway.”

“I’ve heard there’s a River-Night-Club arrangement about here. Very exclusive.”

“We know. Excludes all who can’t crowd in. Come on. Me for wine.”

I found them, having almost broken my shins against a wooden affair, lying grouped on what Shirley said was unmistakably “a sweep of velvet sward.” Venice, it seemed, was exploring. You couldn’t see your hand before your face. But you didn’t want to.

“Funny,” sighed Hugo, “if chap, just any chap, probably quite a nice chap, but timid chap, wakes from sleep to see Venice looking in on him. Mermaid theory.﻿ ﻿…”

“Wot’s this?” snapped a voice. “You’re trespassing.”

===Chinese===

我抓住了她的脚踝。我的手能绕上两圈。

“照顾好他们，”她低声耳语，“亲爱的，照顾好他们。盯紧威尼斯那孩子。她太鲁莽了。快点，追上他们。不知怎的，我只能指望你了——”

“你不能指望我，艾丽丝。我是艾丽丝·斯托姆的敌人。”

“噢，什么朋友敌人的！人指望的是别人的本质，而不管境遇让他们产生何种感觉。”

“那境遇呢，艾丽丝——境遇会让一个女人变得如此无情吗？”

“无情！这词未免太重了。无情？可也许我厌倦了痛苦。所以也许我走进了一座花园，并在周围筑起了一堵高墙。噢，也许吧，也许吧！亲爱的朋友，现在快追他们去吧。我很不安，他们太年轻了。听声音，他们好像游得很远了。……”

但从水面上听来，那些声音仿佛就在耳边一英尺处。我想，他们必定在正前方，朝对岸游去。水流中迅速划过一道耳语，从我身旁掠过。“年轻的偷懒鬼！”盖伊低语道。但我那天晚上在水之波心绝不是为了锻炼身体。我懒洋洋地游着，倾听着前方的声音，隔着水面听来清脆锐利。隐约地、轻柔地、带着湿冷的触感，水草会拂过身体。繁星宛如大军的枪尖，列阵向下压来要惩戒这个世界。但黑暗阻挡了它们，我漂入黑暗的中心，我徘徊流连。

“小心岸壁碰头，威尼斯！威尼斯！喂，威尼斯在哪儿？”

“在这儿。我说，这是什么地方？”

“噢，我的小宝贝们，人为什么不永远泡在水里呢！我说，这个木头玩意儿是什么？”

“依我看像个码头。嗯？我说，雨果，这是什么地方？嗯？”

“我是梅登黑德大学毕业的吗，跑来问我？不过管它呢，咱们上去试试。”

“我听说这附近有个水上夜总会。非常排外。”

“我们懂。把所有挤不进去的人都排斥在外。来吧。我去喝酒。”

我在几乎把胫骨撞在木头上之后找到了他们，他们三三两两地躺在雪莉所谓“毫无疑问是一片丝绒草坪”的地方。威尼斯似乎在四处探险。伸手不见五指。但也没人想看见。

“真逗，”雨果叹道，“要是某个家伙，随便哪个家伙，大概是个挺不错的家伙，但胆子很小，一觉醒来看见威尼斯正探头打量着他。美人鱼理论。……”

“这是干什么？”一个声音厉声喝道，“你们私闯民宅了。”

===Original===

“What did you think we were doing?” Napier asked mildly. “Playing dominoes?”

“Tell us what this place is,” said Shirley severely, “and perhaps we may let you go.”

“Gawd, don’t you know The River Club!”

“I knew it was,” said Hugo proudly, “as soon as I picked up a bus-ticket scented with Bacardie Rum﻿—”

“But where’s Venice?” cried Shirley sharply, “Venice, Oh!”

The darkness stirred, and from the river-edge Napier called on the name of Venice.

“All right, all right! Here I am.”

“Venice!” cried Guy sharply. “Keep straight ahead towards that canoe.”

“I’ll swear,” said Napier, “her voice came a good way from the left. What?”

“From the right, and what to you,” said Hugo.

“From the left,” said Napier, and there was a faint splash and a faint rustle from the water.

“Now, Shirley,” said Guy, “I’ll drown you if you go playing any fancy tricks. Come along, let’s race back.”

“Naps, found her?”

“Oh, she’s only playing the fool!” came Napier’s voice. “Heard her a moment ago. It’s all right.”

I think that Guy and Hugo and Shirley must have deflected rather to one side, for although I was the poorest swimmer of the four I arrived first at the boat. My sight in the darkness was not helped by bumping my head against the gunwale.

“Iris! Iris?”

“Hello, where’s Iris? What? God, it’s dark.﻿ ﻿…”

“But haven’t you found Venice?”

“Oh, she’s playing the fool! Missed her.﻿ ﻿…” We held on to the gunwale, panting. “God, man, where’s Iris? What? I say, Iris!”

“She must have got out to stretch her legs,” I said.

“Yes﻿—God, look!” panted Napier. “What the devil! What?”

Hugo’s voice, Shirley’s, Guy’s.

Napier and I were in the canoe. Iris’s white dress lay anyhow over the cushions in the middle, over the watches and rings. I stumbled over her shoes.

“Oh!” sobbed Shirley. “Something’s happened!”

“Naps, what is it?” snapped Guy from the water.

“Iris﻿—I say, she must have changed her mind and gone in!”

“Stuff, changed her mind! Gone in after Venice, you mean!”

“Iris! Iris!” Hugo called. We all called.

“But where’s Venice?” Shirley screamed just as Napier plunged in again.

“Iris! Venice! Iris!”

“For God’s sake, Naps, take care!” snapped Guy. “Don’t go under that bridge.”

“Iris! Venice! Venice!”

Shirley was sobbing. In the pitch darkness.﻿ ﻿…

“Hugo,” said Guy, “you and I together, for that bridge. Here, this way.﻿ ﻿… Naps, Naps! Come back, you fool!”

“Help.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“你以为我们在干什么？”内皮尔温和地反问，“玩多米诺骨牌吗？”

“告诉我们这是什么地方，”雪莉严厉地说，“也许我们就会放过你。”

“天哪，难道你们不知道‘河畔俱乐部’吗！”

“我就知道是这里，”雨果得意地说，“我一捡到一张散发着百加得朗姆酒香味的公共汽车票就知道了——”

“可是威尼斯在哪儿？”雪莉猛然尖叫起来，“威尼斯，噢！”

黑暗中一阵骚动，从河岸边内皮尔呼喊着威尼斯的名字。

“好啦，好啦！我在这儿呢。”

“威尼斯！”盖伊厉声喊道，“直直朝着那条独木舟游回去。”

“我敢发誓，”内皮尔说，“她的声音是从靠左很远的地方传来的。嗯？”

“是从右边，听你的才怪，”雨果说。

“从左边，”内皮尔说，水面上传来微弱的扑通声和轻微的窸窣声。

“听着，雪莉，”盖伊说，“你要是敢耍花招，我就把你淹死。来吧，咱们比比谁先游回去。”

“纳普斯，找到她了吗？”

“哎，她只是在胡闹！”内皮尔的声音传来，“刚才我还听见她了。没事。”

我想盖伊、雨果和雪莉肯定是偏向了一侧，因为尽管我是四个人中水性最差的一个，我却最先回到了船边。在黑暗中一头撞在船舷上，对我的视力毫无帮助。

“艾丽丝！艾丽丝？”

“喂，艾丽丝在哪儿？嗯？天哪，太黑了。……”

“可你没找到威尼斯吗？”

“哎，她在胡闹呢！没碰上她。……”我们抓住船舷，气喘吁吁。“天哪，伙计，艾丽丝去哪儿了？嗯？我说，艾丽丝！”

“她肯定是上岸去活动腿脚了，”我说。

“是啊——天哪，看！”内皮尔喘着粗气，“搞什么鬼！嗯？”

雨果的声音、雪莉的声音、盖伊的声音。

内皮尔和我跳进了独木舟。艾丽丝的白裙杂乱地堆在中间的垫子上，盖在手表和戒指上面。我被她的鞋子绊了一下。

“噢！”雪莉抽泣起来，“出事了！”

“纳普斯，怎么了？”盖伊在水里厉声喝问。

“艾丽丝——我说，她肯定是改了主意下水了！”

“扯淡，什么改了主意！你是说她下水找威尼斯去了！”

“艾丽丝！艾丽丝！”雨果大喊。我们大家都在喊。

“可是威尼斯在哪儿？”雪莉尖叫道。

===Original===

“God, who’s that! Iris? Venice?”

“Help﻿ ﻿… here, to the right.﻿ ﻿…” An exhausted whisper from the pit of the water.

“It’s Iris,” said Guy. “Where are you, Iris? Here, I’m in the water. Hold on.”

“Quick﻿ ﻿… tired.﻿ ﻿…”

“But where’s Venice?” screamed Shirley.

“All right, Iris has got her.﻿ ﻿…”

Iris’s whisper: “Call Napier back. Oh, dear.﻿ ﻿…”

“Naps! Naps!”

“All right, coming.”

“Hang on, Hugo!” said Guy from the water. “Iris coming. Pull, you fool. I’ve got Venice.”

“Please, my foot.﻿ ﻿…”

“Hugo, don’t capsize the bloody boat!” sobbed Shirley. “Naps, here they are! Guy, give me Venice at once! Venice!”

“All right, Guy.” Was that Venice’s voice? “I can manage.﻿ ﻿…”

“You’ve managed quite enough, you have!” said Hugo. “You all right, Iris?”

Iris was lying panting somewhere in the canoe. Mostly on our flannels, I thought. But you couldn’t see a thing. We were on the quay, Hugo, Guy, and I. Then Napier came. A silent, phantom presence.

“Don’t strike a match, anyone,” Iris whispered. “I’m in my chemise﻿ ﻿… what’s left of it.﻿ ﻿…”

A sob, a jumble, a cry: “Oh, God, Oh, God! I’m so glad to be back!”

“Little donkey!” said Guy. “All right now, Venice?”

“Hugo,” Iris called, very huskily, “where’s that champagne? Venice would like﻿ ﻿… Child, must you breathe your last down my neck?”

“You saved me!” sobbed Venice. “Yes, you did!”

“Ssh!”

“Ow, I was frightened!”

“Like a mouse in the water. Poor Venice.﻿ ﻿…”

“Here’s another towel, Mrs. Storm,” said Shirley brusquely. Shirley would be a little jealous now of Venice liking Iris.﻿ ﻿…

“Listen!” cried Venice into the night. “This woman saved me. Saved my life, she did! ‘Oi!’ said I, and there she was, quick as quick.﻿ ﻿…”

“But, Venice, you’re sitting on my only other stocking, and I’ve only got two!”

“Pop!” said the champagne.

“Have mine, please do! Like barefoot. Jimmy, I got such a bump on the head.”

“How?” Guy asked dangerously.

“Against the bridge, please.﻿ ﻿…”

“That’s only bump (a),” said Hugo kindly. “There’s still bump (b) coming to you, if I heard aright.”

“You did,” said Guy.

“You leave her alone!” snapped Shirley.

“Venice?” Napier’s voice, a white, still voice. He was kneeling, beside me, peering into the canoe. “All right now, Venice?”

“Yes, Naps.” A shy, uncertain voice that was. She was afraid. “You must thank Mrs. Storm for that.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“天哪，那是谁！艾丽丝？威尼斯？”

“救命……在这儿，往右边。……”从深深的水底传来一声精疲力竭的微弱呼救。

“是艾丽丝，”盖伊说，“你在哪儿，艾丽丝？在这儿，我已经下水了。坚持住。”

“快点……没力气了。……”

“可是威尼斯在哪儿？”雪莉尖叫起来。

“没事，艾丽丝抓住她了。……”

一连串的混乱骚动。有人差点被淹死。有人在水里拼命推搡。有人滑倒了，尖叫着，有人把某个人拖进了独木舟里。“别管我，先拉威尼斯！”艾丽丝低语道。接着是更多的水花飞溅，雨果和盖伊把威尼斯拽上了船，威尼斯像一条鱼一样瘫软在船底，喘息着，咳嗽着。

“我只穿着衬裙……剩下的那点碎布。……”

一声抽泣，语无伦次的一声哭喊：“噢上帝啊，噢上帝啊！我真高兴能活着回来！”

“小蠢驴！”盖伊说，“现在没事了吧，威尼斯？”

“雨果，”艾丽丝呼唤道，声音极其沙哑，“那香槟在哪里？威尼斯想喝……孩子，你非要把最后一口气吐在我的脖子上吗？”

“你救了我！”威尼斯抽泣着，“是的，是你救了我！”

“嘘！”

“哎哟，我吓坏了！”

“像水里的一只小老鼠。可怜的威尼斯。……”

“这儿还有一条毛巾，斯托姆夫人，”雪莉语气生硬地说。雪莉此刻大概有点嫉妒威尼斯对艾丽丝的好感了。……

“听着！”威尼斯对着夜空喊道，“这个女人救了我。她救了我的命，真的！我说了一声‘哎哟！’，她就像闪电一样赶到了。……”

“可是，威尼斯，你坐着我仅有的一条备用长袜上了，我总共就只有两条！”

“砰！”香槟酒瓶塞弹开了。

“穿我的吧，请务必穿我的！我喜欢光着脚。吉米，我的头撞得好厉害。”

“怎么撞的？”盖伊危险地问。

“撞在桥上了，拜托。……”

“那只是闷棍 (a)，”雨果好心地说，“要是我的耳朵没听错的话，闷棍 (b) 还在后头候着你呢。”

“你没听错，”盖伊说。

“你不许动她！”雪莉厉声喝道。

“威尼斯？”内皮尔的声音，苍白而静止的声音。他跪在我身旁，窥探着独木舟内。“现在没事了吧，威尼斯？”

“没事了，纳普斯。”那是一个怯生生、不确定的声音。她在害怕。“你必须为此好好谢谢斯托姆夫人。……”

===Original===

Napier did not call on Iris’s name. Hugo chattered to cover the silence. I thought I heard Guy mutter something between his teeth. During the next few minutes Hugo’s dexterity with the champagne was a great relief. Dear Hugo.

“Venice!” said Guy beside me, chill, queerly harsh. “Your health, Venice! You’ll need a good deal, if you go playing any more tricks like that.”

Shirley was saying: “Here’s another towel, Mrs. Storm. Do have my stockings, please.﻿ ﻿…”

“Oh﻿ ﻿… no, it’s quite all right, really. Please, really. But would you mind seeing if my shoes are anywhere there, by the steering-wheel thing?”

Formal, like the voices of women in a drawing-room.

Iris called to me for a cigarette. It was her right hand to which I gave it. It seemed very naked, that right hand. “Your ring, Iris?”

“In the Thames,” she whispered. “Fallen forever! Not a word.﻿ ﻿…”

Venice was explaining to the darkness, gulping lavishly at her champagne: “Thought I’d go for a swim and not just paddle about. Thought I’d be clever. Thought you were fools. Thought I’d thought right. Thought I’d﻿—anyhow, I caught my head crack (a) on that bridge. And then I didn’t want to let out a yell about nothing and look a silly ass. Heard you calling me, but thought I’d better keep my breath for swimming. Began swimming, and got a weed like a wrestler’s torso round me. Head hurt, like hell it did. Thinks I, now for a yell, but began kicking instead﻿—”

“You would!”

“Wait. And my head hurt. And I was frightened to death. And I prayed like fury. Naps! Where’s Naps? I missed you. And when I wanted to yell all I could let out was a miow. And Mrs. Storm﻿—well, Iris, as she saved my life, cries out: ‘Oi, what’s that? Who? Where?’ And before you could say knife, and just as I was succumbing to a watery grave, she was saving my life, quick as you like. Quick, terse stuff. She could swim all of us off our feet, she could.﻿ ﻿…”

“Get very easily tired,” Iris said.

“Iris!” Napier’s voice, sharp. “You dressed? What? Risky for you, messing about, after your illness.”

“I’m almost ready, Napier.” Impatient, Iris’s voice was, I thought.

“Naps, get a rug from the car. She’s shivering.”

“Please!” Iris whispered, frantically, desperately. “For pity’s sake, please not!”

Silence.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

内皮尔没有呼唤艾丽丝的名字。雨果喋喋不休地说着话来掩盖这片沉默。我想我听见盖伊在齿缝间嘟囔了一句什么。在接下来的几分钟里，雨果熟练地倒着香槟，这成了一种极大的解脱。亲爱的雨果。

“威尼斯！”盖伊在我身旁说道，语气冰冷，带着一种古怪的严厉，“敬你的健康，威尼斯！要是你再敢玩这种花招，你可真得需要很多好运才行了。”

雪莉正在说道：“这儿还有一条毛巾，斯托姆夫人。请一定穿上我的长袜。……”

“噢……不，真的完全不用，真的。但是能麻烦你看看我的鞋子是不是在方向盘那边的某个地方吗？”

一本正经，宛如客厅里女人们的客套嗓音。

艾丽丝向我要一支烟。我把烟递到了她的右手。那只右手显得极其光秃。“你的戒指呢，艾丽丝？”

“在泰晤士河里，”她低语道，“永远掉进去了！别声张。……”

威尼斯在大口大口灌着香槟，向着黑夜解释道：“我本来想着去畅游一番，而不只是在水里划划水。我想着自己聪明着呢。我想着你们都是笨蛋。我想着自己想得挺对的。我想着——不管怎样，我的脑袋挨了那座桥狠狠的一记闷棍 (a)。接着我又不想为了鸡毛蒜皮的事大喊大叫显得像个蠢货。我听见你们喊我，但心想还是把力气留着游泳吧。游着游着，一团水草像摔跤手的身躯一样把我死死缠住了。头疼得要命，真他妈疼。我想，这下得喊救命了，但我却拼命蹬水——”

“你向来如此！”

“等等。我的头疼极了。我吓得要死。我发疯般地祈祷。纳普斯！纳普斯在哪儿？我想念你。等我想大声呼救时，所能发出的叫声却像猫叫一样微弱。而斯托姆夫人——好吧，既然她救了我的命，就叫艾丽丝吧——大喊道：‘哎，那是什么？谁在那儿？在哪儿？’还没等你说出‘刀子’这个词呢，就在我快要沉入水底坟墓的时候，她就赶来救了我的命，动作快得不可思议。干脆利落。她能把我们所有人都游得趴下，她真行。……”

“我很容易累的，”艾丽丝说。

“艾丽丝！”内皮尔的声音，严厉尖锐，“你穿好衣服了吗？嗯？大病初愈这样胡闹，对你太冒险了。”

“我差不多穿好了，内皮尔。”艾丽丝的声音听起来有点不耐烦，我想。

“纳普斯，从车里拿条毛毯来。她在发抖。”

“求你了！”艾丽丝低语道，发疯般、绝望地低语，“看在怜悯的份上，千万别拿！”

沉默。……

===Original===

As we collected round the two motorcars, Guy, fiddling about with his starting-arrangements, seemed, I thought, to be saying something. But he was only swearing.

At the back of the Hispano Iris went to sleep against my shoulder. She spoke in her sleep: “You will find me quite light on you, as I haven’t got a chemise. They say it is very smart, to be chemiseless. Already I feel less of an outlaw from society. She did it on purpose.”

“Iris!”

“She did. Half on purpose. I know she did. The pet! Oh, dear.﻿ ﻿…”

“But, Iris, why?”

“Because, dear. So that I should like her.﻿ ﻿…”

“Oh! Well, do you?”

“Yes. Oh, yes.”

“Well?”

“I’m sorry if my hair is tickling your face.”

“Well, now you like her, does it make any difference?”

“No. Oh, no.”

“Oh!”

“Good night.”

===Chinese===

当我们聚在两辆汽车周围时，盖伊摆弄着启动装置，我想，他似乎在说些什么。但他只是在咒骂。

在伊斯帕诺轿车的后座上，艾丽丝靠在我的肩膀上睡着了。她在梦中呓语：“你会觉得我压在你身上挺轻的，因为我没有穿衬裙。他们说不穿衬裙是非常时髦的。我已经觉得自己没那么像社会的叛逆弃儿了。她是故意的。”

“艾丽丝！”

“她是故意的。一半是故意的。我知道她是。那个小宝贝！噢，天哪。……”

“可是，艾丽丝，为什么呢？”

“因为，亲爱的。好让我喜欢她。……”

“噢！那么，你喜欢她吗？”

“喜欢。噢，喜欢。”

“那么呢？”

“如果我的头发弄痒了你的脸，我很抱歉。”

“那么，既然你喜欢她，这会有什么改变吗？”

“没有。噢，没有。”

“噢！”

“晚安。”

## XI / 第十一章 圣乔治为英格兰！
===Original===

St. George for England!

===Chinese===

圣乔治为英格兰！

### I / 第一节
===Original===

Now as I come to that last night of all, a night that was as though set on a stage by a cunning but reckless craftsman of the drama, and as I look every way I may at the happenings that were staged on the platform of that night, I do sincerely thank my stars that it is no novel I have set my hand to, but a faithful chronicle of events. For it would seem that the novelist, so he is an honest man and loves his craft, must work always under a great disadvantage in his earnest wish to tell of life truthfully; since, as the old, old saying is, he never can dare to be so improbable as life. He may, to be sure, be as dingy as life, according to the mode of the day, or he may even achieve the impossible and be more dingy than life, also according to the mode of the day, but to be as improbable as life will be as far beyond the honest novelist’s courage as it must be against the temper of his craft; for should his characters have to “break out,” should the novelist be so far gallant as to concede something to the profligate melodrama of life, his people may only “break out” along lines which the art of their creator has laid out and made inevitable for them; whereas you and I know that living men will do queer things which are desperately alien from what we had thought their possibilities﻿—nay, impossibilities﻿—to be, living men will defy the whole art of characterisation in the twinkling of an eye and destroy every canon of art in a throb of a desire: so that we may make no count or chart of the queer, dark sides of our fellows, nor put any limit, of art, psychology, romance or decency, to the impossibilities which are, within the trembling of a leaf, possible to men and women.

It is not often that I see Venice nowadays, for she lives for the most part with her father in the country, but now and again she will ask me to luncheon in her house in Upper Brook Street, or maybe I will call there on a sudden and find her sitting alone with an unopened book. We do not ever talk of that night, nor of the two chief players of that night, but the other day it came about that I found her sitting absorbed in the shadows of a dying fire, and I somehow said: “Waiting, Venice, waiting!”

===Chinese===

如今，当我回想起那最后一个夜晚——一个仿佛由某位狡黠却放荡不羁的戏剧大师布置在舞台上的夜晚；当我从各个角度审视在那晚的舞台上上演的桩桩事件时，我由衷地感谢上苍，自己着手写下的不是一部虚构小说，而是一部忠实的历史记录。因为看来小说家——只要他是一个诚实的人并热爱自己的行当——在真诚渴望如实描绘生活时，必须始终承受极大的劣势；因为正如那句古老的话所说，他绝不敢像生活本身那样离奇。诚然，根据当下的风尚，他可以像生活一样阴郁沉闷，甚至可以超越不可能、比生活更加阴郁沉闷；但要像生活本身那样不可思议，既远非一个诚实的小说家的勇气所能及，也必然违背他那门手艺的法则；因为哪怕他的角色必须“爆发”，哪怕小说家大度地对生活中肆无忌惮的通俗剧成分做出某种让步，他的人物也只能沿着其创造者的艺术所规划并命定的轨迹去“爆发”；然而你我心里都清楚，活生生的人所做出的怪异之举，往往与我们原本以为的他们的可能性——不，甚至是绝无可能之事——相去甚远；活生生的人会转瞬间违抗整个性格刻画的艺术，会在一念欲望的悸动中摧毁一切艺术规范：以至于我们无法对同类身上阴暗古怪的侧面做出任何统计或图解，也无法用艺术、心理学、传奇或体统来限制男男女女在一叶飘落的刹那间所能化为可能的种种“绝无可能”。

如今我并不常常见到威尼斯，因为她大部分时间都和父亲一起住在乡下，但偶尔她会请我去她在上布鲁克街的宅邸吃午饭，或者我也许会突然登门造访，发现她独自坐着，面前放着一本未曾翻开的书。我们从不谈论那个夜晚，也从不谈论那一夜的两个主要主角，但前几天，我碰巧发现她凝视着渐渐熄灭的炉火阴影出神，我不知怎地脱口而出：“在等待着，威尼斯，在等待着！”

===Original===

She was crouched like a child in the gloom of a Dorothy chair, and as I sat in another nearby a friendly flame darted through the twilight and made toys of her eyes. They were looking at me with every appearance of deep reflection, but now it was a woman who was looking out at me from Venice’s eyes, and the woman seemed to smile, and she said: “He is in India, with Bruce’s expedition. He will be coming home soon.”

And then for the first time we spoke of that tempestuous night in July, the night but one after the children’s party. But of course I did not tell Venice all, particularly about the last part, according to the promise sworn between Sir Maurice Harpenden, Hilary and me.

My clock was about to strike nine o’clock, as I very well remember for I had nothing to do but stare at it, when the telephone-bell beat it, may I say, by a short head, and Iris’s voice said:

“Is that you?”

“And who should it be,” I said, “but me? I am so glad you rang up, Iris.”

“Oh, you are lonely!”

They shout on the telephone, people do, so that one cannot always hear them very, very well. But this fell lady’s slightly husky voice was considerate and clear.

“But fancy,” she said, “finding you at home now, and all the world at dinner or the play! Dear, are you, too, a social outcast? I am so sorry you have had to dine alone.”

“Iris, you should have brought up the friends of your childhood to a better understanding of the arts of peace. I was to have dined with Hilary tonight, and because of my engagement with him I did not go to a dinner where I was to sit beside a woman who has studied the Yogi philosophies and was divorced last year in New York with nine corespondents, the tenth being disqualified on the ground that he was a black man weighing seventeen stone in his boots. And then Ross rings me up at half-past seven to say that Hilary has been called to the country!”

“Yes, I knew you had been put off for dinner. I was so shocked.”

“Thank you. But, Iris, you knew?”

“Oh, I know everything! But listen, I am ringing you up to ask you a plain question, and I would like, please, a plain answer. Does it mean anything to you that I am leaving England tomorrow at dawn?”

“You depress me, Iris Storm.”

“But I, oh I am so gay!”

===Chinese===

她像个孩子一样蜷缩在多萝西扶手椅的阴影里，当我在旁边的另一张椅子坐下时，一抹友善的火苗穿透薄暮，将她的眼眸映照得宛如玩具。她的目光带着深思熟虑的神态看着我，但此刻从威尼斯的眼眸深处望向我的，却是一个成熟的女人，那个女人似乎微笑了，她说：“他人在印度，参加布鲁斯的探险队。他很快就要回家了。”

接着，我们第一次谈起了七月那个风雨飘摇的夜晚，也就是孩子们聚会之后的第二晚。但当然，按照莫里斯·哈彭登爵士、希拉里和我之间立下的誓言，我并未将一切和盘托出，特别是关于最后的那个部分。

我的钟正准备敲响九点，我记得很清楚，因为我当时无事可做，只能盯着它看，这时电话铃声可以说以半个马头的微弱优势抢先响了，艾丽丝的声音传来：

“是你吗？”

“除了我，”我说，“还能是谁呢？真高兴你打电话来，艾丽丝。”

“噢，你很寂寞吧！”

人们在电话里总爱大喊大叫，以至于人们往往听不太真切。但这位凶险女士略带沙哑的声音却格外体贴清晰。

“可真想不到，”她说，“这个时间竟然能把你堵在家里，全天下的人都在吃晚饭或看戏呢！亲爱的，难道你也是个被社交界遗弃的人吗？真遗憾你不得不一个人吃晚饭。”

“艾丽丝，你真该把你童年的朋友们培养得更懂和平的艺术。我今晚原本是要和希拉里一起吃饭的，正因为和他有约，我推掉了一场晚宴——在那场晚宴上我本该坐在一个研究瑜伽哲学、去年在纽约离婚时列出九名通奸共犯的女人身旁，第十名被取消了资格，理由是他是一个穿着靴子体重达十七英石的黑人。然后罗斯在七点半打电话给我，说希拉里被叫去乡下了！”

“是的，我知道你的晚饭被推掉了。我感到非常难过。”

“谢谢你。可是，艾丽丝，你怎么知道的？”

“噢，我什么都知道！但听着，我打电话给你是想问你一个坦白的问题，我希望听到一个坦白的回答。我明天黎明离开英国，这对你意味着什么吗？”

“你让我感到沮丧，艾丽丝·斯托姆。”

“可是我，噢我快乐极了！”

===Original===

“Yes, that is what depresses me. My friends are wretched, but you are gay! Iris, we are all of us miserable sinners, but you are a very captain of wickedness. Iris, you are a wrecker of homes, and you say you are gay! I am not being flippant. I have dined alone.”

“Dear, I understand. I do respect your disapproval, you must believe me, or else I would answer that we begin to die when we are born, that all comes from God and goes to the devil, and so what does anything matter? But listen, O father and brother of disapproval, would you like to see me before I leave England tomorrow at dawn?”

“Yes,” I said, “I would.”

“ ‘See’ me, I said, not ‘murder’ me!”

“But, Iris, I can qualify nothing tonight!”

“My idea is to take you into the country tonight. We go à deux. We go into a darkness. My friend, there is a sundial in a certain garden, and it is written that you and I shall stand by that sundial before we part to meet nevermore.”

“Iris, your voice is laughing, but you are not laughing. What does that mean?”

“But I am afraid! I am laughing with fear.﻿ ﻿…”

“And we are driving into the country to escape your fear?”

“Oh, but that hurts! I was never before accused of being a coward.﻿ ﻿…”

“Iris, I’m sorry.”

“Sir Maurice Harpenden knows me better than you do, my friend. Ah, he is very clever, is Sir Maurice! But you will see. We are driving into the country, let me tell you, to meet my fear. And when we meet it I shall not mock, nor tremble, nor quail, but I shall be a very Saint George for steadfastness. That is the programme, so far. And you, will you be my esquire?”

“You speak of darkness, of sundials, of fear, of Sir Maurice Harpenden, whom I do not know, of Saint George of Cappadocia, whom, alas, one sees only too little of these days. I think that you, too, must have dined alone. And you have gone mad. Else why must we drive into the country?”

“But we go to keep high company tonight, that’s why! Are you afraid of *that*? The captains and the kings of the countryside are our adversaries. Sweet, you and I shall stand arrayed against the warriors of conduct.”

“Not I, Iris! I am for conduct.”

“You lie, dear. You are for love! Oh, why do you lie?”

“Because one must be reasonable, Iris.”

===Chinese===

“是的，这正是我沮丧的原因。我的朋友们痛苦万分，你却欢欣雀跃！艾丽丝，我们大家都是可怜的罪人，可你简直就是罪恶的统帅。艾丽丝，你是个家庭破坏者，你却说你快乐！我不是在开玩笑。我是一个人吃晚饭的。”

“亲爱的，我理解。我尊重你的不赞同，你必须相信我，否则我就会反驳说，我们生来就走向死亡，一切皆来自上帝又归于魔鬼，那么任何事情又有什么大不了的呢？但是听着，不赞同的化身啊，在明天黎明我离开英国之前，你想见见我吗？”

“想，”我说，“我想。”

“我说的是‘见见’我，而不是‘谋杀’我！”

“可是，艾丽丝，我今晚什么保证都给不了！”

“我的想法是今晚带你去乡下一趟。就我们两个人。我们走向黑暗。我的朋友，在某座花园里有一座日晷，上面写着你和我将在立誓永不复相见之前，一同伫立在那座日晷旁。”

“艾丽丝，你的声音在笑，但你并不是在笑。这是什么意思？”

“可我害怕啊！我是因为恐惧才笑的。……”

“而我们开车去乡下是为了逃避你的恐惧？”

“噢，这话可真伤人！以前从没有人指责我是个胆小鬼。……”

“艾丽丝，对不起。”

“莫里斯·哈彭登爵士比你更了解我，我的朋友。啊，莫里斯爵士可精明着呢！但你会看到的。我告诉你，我们开车去乡下，是为了迎面直面我的恐惧。当我们直面它时，我既不会嘲弄，也不会战栗退缩，而会像圣乔治一样坚定不移。到目前为止，日程安排就是这样。而你，愿意做我的侍从骑士吗？”

“你提到了黑暗、日晷、恐惧，提到了我素未谋面的莫里斯·哈彭登爵士，提到了卡帕多细亚的圣乔治——唉，这年头人们太少见到他了。我想，你肯定也是一个人吃的晚饭。而且你已经疯了。否则我们为什么非得开车去乡下？”

“可我们今晚是去会见高贵的贵客啊，所以才去！你害怕那个吗？乡间的统帅与君王是我们的对手。亲爱的，你和我将列队迎战行为准则的捍卫勇士。”

“我可不去，艾丽丝！我站在准则这一边。”

“你撒谎，亲爱的。你站在爱情这一边！噢，你为什么要撒谎呢？”

“因为人必须理智，艾丽丝。”

===Original===

“Oh, because this, because that, because of the persecution of men, the savagery of beasts, the malice of gods! Free me of your becauses! Lies, all lies! One must be truthful, there is no other law, all other laws are lies. We are educated by lies, we live with lies, we worship lies, we fight for lies, we die bearded with lies. God made men out of clay, countries out of mud, and what can the son of a marriage between clay and mud be but Master Lie? Oh, let us have just one look at the Demoiselle Truth!”

“Unfortunately, Iris, that demoiselle shows a different figure to all of us. Now I may like her with a trivial ankle, tawny hair, boyish breasts, but another may like her with golden hair and spacious loins, as Rubens painted women﻿—”

“I will say this for you, that when you insult one you do it with kindness. It is kind of you to have described me as your idea of the demoiselle. I am proud of my breasts, because they are so beautiful. Life is generally so rude to a woman’s breasts, but it has only kissed mine﻿—”

“Iris, you are shocking the girl at the exchange!”

“No, no, Miss Dell has prepared her for anything! But you haven’t yet said if you will be my esquire into the country? Why are you so silent?”

“But, Iris, I don’t understand a word of this!”

“Sweet, do we need to wait on your understanding! Chivalry?”

“Away with that from me to you! You always chose the man’s part.”

“Gallantry?”

“But I shall be gallant to another in being ungallant to you!”

“Friendship?”

“You are driving me very hard, Iris. I do not want to say what is in my mind.”

“Can you stand there with your lips to the receiver, which I hope your servants keep clean for you, and tell me you are not my friend? Can you stand there facing me across Queen Street, Curzon Street, Hertford Street, Hamilton Place, Hyde Park Corner and Knightsbridge, and tell me that you are not my friend? I am sitting here on the edge of the bed, in the next room is Mrs. Oden trying to pretend she is not listening to every word I say, all round me are trunks and boxes, about me is a leather jacket with a collar of a few minks, and on my head is one green hat. Are you not my friend? Answer me! Answer me, I say! Dear, a woman must have one friend! It is usual.”

“But the emerald is gone, Iris. So you are not the Iris I knew. You were Iris Storm, you are Iris March, and I never have met Iris March.”

===Chinese===

“噢，因为这个，因为那个，因为世人的迫害，野兽的凶残，诸神的恶意！别拿你的‘因为’来烦我了！全是谎言，全都是谎言！人必须真诚坦白，世上再无别的法则，其余一切法则全是谎言。我们在谎言中受教育，我们与谎言共存，我们崇拜谎言，我们为谎言而战，我们死时胡须上挂满了谎言。上帝用泥土造人，用污泥造国家，泥土与污泥结合的产物除了‘谎言老爷’还能是什么？噢，让我们哪怕瞧一眼‘真理小姐’吧！”

“遗憾的是，艾丽丝，那位小姐展现在我们每个人面前的形象都大相径庭。比如我可能喜欢她长着纤巧的脚踝、黄褐色的秀发、男孩般的胸脯；但别人可能喜欢她长着金发和丰硕的臀胯，正如鲁本斯画中的女人那样——”

“关于你我必须承认这一点：你侮辱人的时候总是带着体贴。你把我描述成你心目中的真理小姐，真是太体贴了。我为自己的胸脯感到骄傲，因为它们是如此美丽。生活对女人的胸脯通常是如此粗暴，但它对我的胸脯却只留下过吻——”

“艾丽丝，你把电话局的接线员姑娘吓坏了！”

“不，不，戴尔小姐的小说早就让她见怪不怪了！但你还没说到底愿不愿意做我的侍从骑士去乡下呢？你为什么这么沉默？”

“可是，艾丽丝，这一切我一个字都没听懂！”

“亲爱的，我们难道非得等你想明白吗！骑士精神呢？”

“从我对你而言，休提那个！你向来喜欢扮演男人的角色。”

“风度呢？”

“要是我对你有风度，那就是对别人有失风度！”

“友谊呢？”

“你逼得我太紧了，艾丽丝。我不想说出我心里所想的话。”

“你敢手拿着听筒——我希望你的佣人把它擦得很干净——站在那里告诉我你不是我的朋友吗？你敢隔着女王街、柯曾街、赫特福德街、汉密尔顿广场、海德公园角和骑士桥面对着我，告诉我你不是我的朋友吗？我就坐在床沿上，隔壁房间的奥登夫人正假装没在偷听我说的每一个字，我身边到处是箱子和大行李，我身上穿着一件领子缀着几根水貂毛的皮夹克，头上戴着一顶绿礼帽。你难道不是我的朋友吗？回答我！我说，回答我！亲爱的，女人必须有一个朋友！向来如此。”

“可是祖母绿已经没了，艾丽丝。所以你已经不是我认识的那个艾丽丝了。你曾经是艾丽丝·斯托姆，现在你是艾丽丝·马奇，而我从未见过艾丽丝·马奇。”

===Original===

“The emerald was wise. There’s a *galanterie* in jewels unknown to men, I see that. So you won’t come for a drive with me? Our last?”

“I never said that, Iris!”

“Ah, I have frightened him! Well, I will come round for you in five minutes. How are you dressed? In black and white? Maybe I would have preferred you in something less formal, in something more﻿—”

“Enough of *pour le sport*, Iris! Oh, enough, enough!”

===Chinese===

“那颗祖母绿是明智的。珠宝身上有着某种男人们未曾领略的风度，我明白这一点。这么说，你是不肯陪我开车兜风了？我们最后的一次？”

“我可没那么说，艾丽丝！”

“啊，我把他吓着了！好吧，我五分钟后开车到你楼下接你。你穿什么衣服？黑白相间的？也许我更希望你穿得别那么正式，穿得更……”

“别再提‘做运动’了，艾丽丝！噢，够了，够了！”

### II / 第二节
===Original===

And so we were again, again and for the last time, in that swift motorcar, wrapped in the gentle silences of the night. The oppression of the heat was gone since the rains of yesterday, but even yet London could not quite rouse itself from the stupor of the past tropical week. And tonight the flight of the stork did not torment the hosts of the midgets, “for,” said Iris from the shadow of her green hat, “there is no hurry, no hurry at all.”

A clock in the High Street of Kensington was at a little after half-past nine o’clock. The wide sweep of road towards Olympia was quiet with the gentle traffic of no-man’s-hour, for such is a little after half-past nine o’clock. I said: “I do wish you would tell me what all this is about.”

“It begins a long time ago, it is a long story. Having to do with the loves of babes, the wisdom of sucklings, and the sins of the fathers. And the sins of the fathers. But I will tell you more when we come to Harrod’s.”

“But we passed Harrod’s long ago!”

“There is another. You will see. Patience.” Through Hammersmith and Chiswick, by Ranelagh and Roehampton, we sped into the veiled countryside. The glow of London was a yellow arch in the night behind. We passed the last omnibus on its last journey to a far-flung corner of the town.

“But,” I pleaded, “I don’t even know where we are going to!”

“Why, to Sutton Marle! Didn’t I tell you? It’s not far.﻿ ﻿…”

“But I don’t know Sir Maurice! Really, Iris, how dare you let me in for this?”

“It is all right, dear, you are expected. I said to Hilary, not an hour ago on the telephone: ‘I am not for Sutton Marle unless I may bring my one friend.’ ”

“Well, I never heard such cheek! And why, Iris, am I your one friend?”

“Because once upon a time you shamed me of my shame. Because you did not hold me cheap. Because romance dies hard in you. Because, dear, I rather like you. And that is why I told Hilary that you were my friend and that I would not dare Sutton Marle without you, adding that as he had put you off for dinner it would be something for you to do.”

“Iris, you are laughing all the time, you who told me you were afraid!”

She glanced at me just then, and that second’s smile is like a wound on my memory. A car screamed and passed us, and she cried through the disordered air: “I am afraid, but of course I am gay, too! Haven’t I waited twelve years for my inheritance!”

===Chinese===

于是我们再次，再次并且是最后一次，坐进了那辆飞驰的机动车里，被夜晚温柔的寂静所包裹。自昨天的降雨之后，酷热的压迫感已经消退，但伦敦依然未能完全从过去一周热带般的麻木中清醒过来。而今晚鹳鸟的飞驰并没有折磨成群的飞虫，“因为，”艾丽丝在绿礼帽的阴影下说道，“不用赶时间，一点都不用赶。”

肯辛顿高街的一座大钟指针刚过九点半。通往奥林匹亚的宽阔道路上只有空档时刻平缓的车流，因为九点半刚过正是这样的时刻。我说：“我真希望你能告诉我这一切究竟是怎么回事。”

“这始于很久很久以前，这是一个漫长的故事。关系到幼童的爱恋，乳儿的智慧，以及父辈的罪孽。父辈的罪孽。但等我们到了哈罗德百货，我会告诉你更多。”

“可我们早就过了哈罗德了！”

“还有另一个呢。你会看到的。耐心点。”穿过哈默史密斯和奇斯威克，经过雷内拉和罗汉普顿，我们疾驰驶入夜幕笼罩的乡村。伦敦的灯火在身后的夜空中化作一道黄色的拱门。我们掠过了开往城市偏远角落的最后一班双层公共汽车。

“但是，”我恳求道，“我甚至都不知道我们要去哪儿！”

“哎，去萨顿·马尔啊！我没告诉你吗？不远。……”

“可我根本不认识莫里斯爵士！真的，艾丽丝，你怎么敢把我卷进这档子事里？”

“没事的，亲爱的，他们正等着你呢。不到一个小时前我在电话里对希拉里说：‘除非允许我带上我唯一的朋友，否则我是不会去萨顿·马尔的。’”

“哎，我从没听过这么厚颜无耻的话！凭什么，艾丽丝，我成了你唯一的朋友？”

“因为很久以前你曾因我的耻辱而让我感到羞愧。因为你没有看轻我。因为传奇的情怀在你心中难以泯灭。因为，亲爱的，我相当喜欢你。这就是为什么我告诉希拉里你是我的朋友，没有你我不敢去萨顿·马尔，我还补充说，既然他推掉了你的晚饭，这正好给你找点事做。”

“艾丽丝，你一直在笑，刚才你还告诉我你很害怕呢！”

就在那时她瞥了我一眼，那一刹那的微笑犹如一道伤口刻在我的记忆里。一辆轿车尖叫着从我们身旁超过去，她迎着混乱的夜风喊道：“我很害怕，但当然我也很快乐！为了我的继承权，难道我没有等待了整整十二年吗！”

===Original===

The flame of the lights on the road ahead made a wall of blackness on each side of us. I was like a child in this blackness, and it seemed to me that her voice was the voice of the night. I did not know what to say. I said: “Iris, that girl will die without Napier.”

Minutes later, she said: “If people died of love I must have risen from the dead to be driving this car now!”

“Indeed, Iris, how can I argue about love against your experience!”

“My friend, you can’t shame me! For I am shame itself come to life. Yes, I have lit many small fires to quench one large fire. I have been unsuccessful. Thank God, thank God for that! And now let the one large fire burn, with a boy and a girl of eighteen for fuel. Nothing else matters.”

“My dear, so much else matters! Restraints, nobilities, decencies, sacrifices!”

We passed slowly through a village High Street, hailed and mocked good-naturedly by a group of men emerging from an inn.

She said: “In the ancient love-tales and the songs of the Jongleurs we read of maidens sacrificed on the altar of circumstance. I was a maiden, even I, once upon a time. Dear, I am afraid you must take my word for that. And I, a maid, was sacrificed to the vulgar ambitions of a Sir Maurice. So let us not talk of sacrifice. It makes me sick with anger.”

Not fast, not slow, the Hispano-Suiza swept through Surrey. Then she said sharply: “But if Venice had had a child!”

I could not see her face, for her hat and the darkness were between us. But ever so faintly I could see her mouth, and her lips were parted, as though she was praying. I wondered if she was praying to whom she could be praying. “She has a God,” had said that captain of men.

“And why did you say that so bitterly, Iris?”

“Was I bitter? Oh, that’s a sin, to think of that angel and to be bitter.”

“Angel? Did you say angel?”

“I said angel,” she said, and no other answer had I but from the stork crying dolorously to warn corners of our flight.

===Chinese===

前方的车灯在公路两侧筑起了一堵黑暗的墙。在这片黑暗中我仿佛变成了一个孩子，在我听来她的声音仿佛就是黑夜本身的声音。我不知道该说什么。我说：“艾丽丝，没有内皮尔，那个姑娘会死的。”

过了几分钟，她说：“要是人真会为爱而死，我现在能开着这辆车必定是从死人堆里爬出来的！”

“确实，艾丽丝，面对你的阅历，我怎么能在爱情上跟你争辩呢！”

“我的朋友，你羞辱不了我！因为我本身就是活生生的耻辱。是的，我曾点燃过许多微弱的火苗，想去扑灭那一团熊熊烈火。我失败了。感谢上帝，为此感谢上帝！而现在就让那一团熊熊烈火燃烧吧，用一个十八岁的少男和一个十八岁的少女作为燃料。其余一切都不重要。”

“亲爱的，其余那么多事情都很重要！克制、高贵、体统、牺牲！”

我们缓缓穿过村庄的高街，从一家小客栈里走出来的一群男人善意地向我们打招呼起哄。

她说：“在古老的爱情传说和吟游诗人的歌谣中，我们读到过在际遇的祭坛上被牺牲的少女。很久以前我也曾是个少女，连我也曾是。亲爱的，恐怕在这点上你必须相信我的话。而我，一个少女，却被牺牲在莫里斯爵士庸俗的野心之下。所以千万别跟我谈什么牺牲。这让我恶心得发狂。”

不快也不慢，伊斯帕诺-苏莎轿车穿过萨里郡。接着她尖锐地说道：“但要是威尼斯生了个孩子呢！”

我看不清她的脸，因为她的帽子和黑暗阻隔在我们之间。但我能极其隐约地看见她的嘴，她的双唇微启，仿佛在祈祷。我纳闷她是在祈祷吗，她又能向谁祈祷呢。“她心中有上帝，”那位领袖人物曾这么说过。

“你为什么说得这么苦涩，艾丽丝？”

“我很苦涩吗？噢，一想到那个天使就心生苦涩，这真是一桩罪过。”

“天使？你说的是天使吗？”

“我说的是天使，”她说，除了车头鹳鸟凄凉地啼鸣以向拐弯处警示我们的飞驰之外，我再没有得到任何回答。

===Original===

“If Venice,” she said reasonably, “had had a child, I would have called to Napier in vain. We can’t know the beginning and end of honour, nor what it is, nor what it will do, nor what will debauch it, nor what will make it unbending as iron. Let us say I have debauched Napier’s honour. Oh, let us say anything! We don’t stand on words on ultimate nights like these. Honour is like a little child, let us say, and like a little child it may be led away by a shining toy, and in this case I am the shining toy. But had Venice had a child I might have shone like Aldebaran and called Napier in vain. And that would have been right and just. We must all give way before children, always, always. Oh, if people had always done that, what miseries wouldn’t the world have been spared! Those whose dreams are clean must give way to children, for babies will carry clean dreams further than the wisest of old men, and slowly the world will rise above the age of smoke and savagery.﻿ ﻿…”

“But it’s absurd, Iris! What chance has the girl had of having a child yet!”

“But I am not pretending to play fair! Or did you think I was? I awoke from my illness, and I awoke suddenly to life. Awaking, I took my chance as it came. And quickly, quickly, for fear of giving Venice a longer chance. And it’s because I haven’t played fair that I am going to Sir Maurice’s house now.”

“Oh!” I said. “Good God! Let me out of this car, Iris! I will walk back to London.”

“Napier doesn’t know. Napier would be frantic if he knew. Napier is dining with Venice tonight. They would both be frantic if they knew I had taken Sir Maurice’s challenge and gone down to Sutton Marle. But I must go, to make unfairness a little less unfair. I must let Sir Maurice have his last joy of me. Besides, there is a fascination in letting men tell the truth to one. There is a fascination in wondering if it will ever be the truth. But look! Oh, look! There is Harrod’s!”

The car had pulled up on the brow of a small hill. The lights searched across the road into an unhedged field. Iris pointed along the flame of light.

“There is Harrod’s,” she said gravely.

“But where is Harrod’s? I see a field and what looks like a giant oak.”

“That is Harrod’s. Not an oak, but an ash. It is very old, and smells of fairies and moonlight.”

There were once two roads that led away from a certain tree.

===Chinese===

“要是威尼斯，”她通情达理地说，“生了个孩子，我呼唤内皮尔也是徒劳。我们不可能知晓荣誉的起点和终点，不知道它究竟是什么，不知道它会做些什么，不知道是什么会腐蚀它，也不知道是什么会让它如生铁般宁折不弯。就算是我腐蚀了内皮尔的荣誉吧。噢，怎么说都行！在这样的终局之夜，我们不会咬文嚼字。荣誉就像一个小孩子，姑且这么说吧，就像一个小孩子会被闪闪发光的玩具引诱走，而在这种情况下我就是那个发光的玩具。但如果威尼斯有了孩子，哪怕我像毕宿五那样光芒四射，呼唤内皮尔也是徒劳的。而那才是公正合理的。我们所有人都必须在孩子面前退让，永远如此，永远如此。噢，要是人们一直都这么做，世间该免去多少苦难啊！那些怀抱纯洁梦想的人必须在孩子面前退让，因为孩童比最睿智的老人能把纯洁的梦想带得更远，世界终将慢慢超越烟火与蒙昧的野蛮时代。……”

“但这也太荒唐了，艾丽丝！那个姑娘到现在才有多少机会生孩子啊！”

“可我根本没打算公平竞争！难道你以为我是吗？我从病榻上苏醒过来，突然向生命苏醒。醒来之后，我抓住了一切降临的机会。而且动作飞快，飞快，生怕给威尼斯更多的机会。正因为我没有公平竞争，我现在才要去莫里斯爵士的宅邸。”

“噢！”我说，“我的天哪！让我下车，艾丽丝！我要走回伦敦。”

“内皮尔不知道。内皮尔要是知道了会发疯的。内皮尔今晚在陪威尼斯吃饭。他们要是知道我接受了莫里斯爵士的挑战、来到了萨顿·马尔，他们俩都会发疯的。但我必须去，好让不公变得稍微没那么不公。我必须让莫里斯爵士从我身上享受他最后的快意。此外，让男人们对你吐露真相自有一种迷人之处。去琢磨那究竟会不会是真相自有一种迷人之处。但是看！噢，看哪！那里就是哈罗德百货！”

轿车在一座小山顶上停了下来。车灯扫过道路，照亮了一片没有树篱遮挡的田野。艾丽丝顺着光柱指过去。

“那就是哈罗德百货，”她严肃地说。

“可哈罗德百货在哪儿呢？我只看见一片田野和一棵看似巨大的橡树。”

“那就是哈罗德。不是橡树，是白蜡树。它很老很老了，散发着仙子和月光的气息。”

曾经有两条路从一棵树分岔开去。

===Original===

The tree, a solitary giant of enormous girth, stood perhaps twenty yards from the road. Its trunk dammed the far-flung eyes of the car, and in the light its leaves were made of silver, and you fancied that, had there been a breath of wind, it had spoken from its ancient wisdom, of which this night stood so sorely in need; but never a whisper stirred the countryside.

“Iris, doesn’t that make your passions look﻿ ﻿… silly?”

She took my hand, and lifted it, and dropped it. I do not know why she did that. Her face was hidden. It seemed to me that a long time passed before she spoke, and I seemed to think of many things.

===Chinese===

那棵树是一个周长巨大的孤独巨人，伫立在距离公路大约二十码处。它的树干挡住了轿车投向远方的目光，在灯光下它的树叶宛如银子铸就，你会觉得，哪怕有一丝微风吹过，它就会吐露它那古老的智慧——而这个夜晚正迫切需要这份智慧；然而整个乡村连一丝耳语都没有掠过。

“艾丽丝，这难道没让你的激情显得……可笑吗？”

她握住我的手，抬起来，又放下了。我不知道她为什么这么做。她的面孔隐藏在阴影中。在我看来过了很长很长时间她才开口，而在那段漫长的时间里我似乎想到了许多事情。

===Original===

“If there was a moon,” she said at last, “a little way behind Harrod’s you would see a small hill, and on the hill you would see a white house. That is where Gerald and I were born. Perhaps Gerald knows why now. That is one of the many things Napier and I have to talk out in the solitudes, why all we men and women are born. There must be a reason. Across the fields this way is Sutton Marle, where Napier was born. We used to play beneath this tree, Gerald, Boy, Napier and I. Boy was older than us, and bossed us. So there was a revolt, and then we made two camps, Boy and Gerald, Napier and I. Sometimes Aunt Eve, who took care of Gerald and me when mother died, would take us all up to London, and we would have tea at Harrod’s. Napier and I loved Harrod’s because we at once got lost there. And so we called this tree Harrod’s, because we were happy here, too. We were twelve then. Later on they discouraged our being together. Aunt Eve didn’t want me to be made miserable when I grew up by not being allowed to marry Napier, for she knew that I didn’t come into Sir Maurice’s plans. Poor Maurice, I’ve crashed into them now, haven’t I! Father got poorer, we sold this house, and went to live in Cambridge Square. Napier was not allowed to see me any more, but we managed to meet somehow. Gerald helped, Aunt Eve helped, Boy helped. That was when Boy first loved me, he said later, because of my determination not to lose Napier. But Sir Maurice won. I was stronger than Napier, but I was not so strong as Sir Maurice. He wanted Napier to marry a rich girl, and Iris March was only the daughter of the younger branch of a bankrupt house. One day, when I was eighteen, I got a wire from Napier to meet him here at Harrod’s that afternoon. I borrowed the money for a taxi﻿—bit from Boy, bit from Hilary﻿—and here Napier was, white, desperate. In a general cleanup before going up to Oxford he had promised his father never to see me again. ‘I like Iris,’ Sir Maurice had said, ‘but she comes of rotten stock.’ I don’t think we had ever realised before that we were in love. I suppose I grew up in that one second. But Napier was still a boy of eighteen, while I was suddenly as old as the Queen of Hearts. I told him I loved him. Dear, I have known many men, I have married two, but I have only told one that I loved him, and he was a boy. Poor Napier, so torn, white, distracted.

===Chinese===

“如果有月亮的话，”良久她终于开口，“在哈罗德百货后头不远，你会看到一座小山丘，山丘上有一栋白房子。那就是杰拉尔德和我出生的地方。也许杰拉尔德现在明白究竟为什么了。那是内皮尔和我必须在旷野孤寂中彻底弄清楚的诸多事情之一：我们所有这些男男女女究竟为何而生。总该有个理由。穿过这边的田野就是萨顿·马尔，内皮尔出生的地方。我们以前常在这棵树下玩耍，杰拉尔德、‘男孩’、内皮尔和我。‘男孩’比我们大，总对我们发号施令。于是就有了反抗，接着我们分成了两个阵营：‘男孩’和杰拉尔德一边，内皮尔和我一边。有时候，在我们母亲去世后照顾杰拉尔德和我的伊夫姑姑，会带我们所有人去伦敦，我们会在哈罗德百货喝下午茶。内皮尔和我最喜欢哈罗德百货，因为我们在那里总会立刻迷路。所以我们把这棵树叫做‘哈罗德’，因为我们在这里也曾那么快乐。当时我们十二岁。后来他们不赞成我们呆在一起。伊夫姑姑不希望我长大后因为不被允许嫁给内皮尔而痛苦不堪，因为她知道我根本不在莫里斯爵士的规划之内。可怜的莫里斯，现在我可是狠狠撞碎了他的全盘计划，不是吗！后来父亲越来越穷，我们卖掉了这栋房子，搬去剑桥广场住。内皮尔不被允许再见我，但我们总能设法碰面。杰拉尔德帮忙，伊夫姑姑帮忙，‘男孩’也帮忙。那是‘男孩’第一次爱上我的时候，他后来这么说的，就因为我无论如何也不肯放弃内皮尔的那股倔强劲儿。但莫里斯爵士赢了。我比内皮尔坚强，但我没有莫里斯爵士坚强。他想让内皮尔娶一个富家千金，而艾丽丝·马奇只是一个破产门第旁支的女儿。有一天，在我十八岁的时候，我收到了内皮尔的一封电报，约我那天下午在‘哈罗德’这里碰面。我借了叫出租车的钱——从‘男孩’那里借了一点，从希拉里那里借了一点——内皮尔就在这里，脸色惨白，绝望至极。在去牛津大学报到前的一场全面清理中，他向他父亲许下誓言，永远不再见我。‘我喜欢艾丽丝，’莫里斯爵士曾说，‘但她出身于恶劣败坏的家族。’我想在此之前我们从没意识到我们彼此相爱。我想就在那一秒钟里我彻底长大了。但内皮尔依然是个十八岁的男孩，而我却突然变得像红桃王后一样沧桑。我告诉他我爱他。亲爱的，我认识过很多男人，结过两次婚，但我只对一个人说过我爱他，而他当年还是个男孩子。可怜的内皮尔，那么痛苦、苍白、心烦意乱。

===Original===

Afraid of my love, which seemed to him almost unholy, afraid of his father, who seemed to him almost holy. England, my England! His father was strong in Napier, and the Harpendens were strong in him. They were stronger than me at eighteen, and they were stronger than the sweet memories of Harrod’s. I said to Napier then, just over there where the lights fall by that trunk, I said, eighteen to eighteen: ‘Napier, I think I have a body that burns for love. Napier, I shall burn it with love, but I never will say “I love you” to any man but you, because it never will be true.’ And what I said at eighteen is true now at thirty, I have never said I loved him to any man but Napier, for it hasn’t been true. I have given myself, in disdain, in desire, with disgust, with delight, but I have kept to that silly, childish boast of mine. I say that to my shame, but now shame is a weed under my foot. I married because my body was hungry for love and born to love and must love. And I thought I would destroy my body with love’s delight, but all I did was to destroy a good man. Hector Storm went off to Ireland and died because one night in my sleep I whispered Napier’s name. Or perhaps I had whispered it many nights. I told him that he was being jealous of a ghost, but he wouldn’t believe. Now all those things are passed. The nymph unloosened her girdle to desire, and now she has unloosened it to her only love. One grows out of everything, even desire: and then one can love. Look, Harrod’s is smiling, all silver and smiling! Here Sir Maurice sacrificed me twelve years ago. Tonight I have to say to him: ‘This is what you have done, Maurice﻿—the unhappiness of Venice, the unhappiness of your son, and twelve years of hell for me. Are you content?’ Oh God, it’s been hell, these twelve years! If you had kissed hell, as I have kissed hell, if you had sacrificed to hell, as I have sacrificed my body to hell, you would know what I mean. But now I can’t grudge Maurice the final satisfaction of telling me what he thinks of me. Dear, it matters so little what men like Maurice think of one. They worship all that is despicable, they despise all that is really good. From the beginning of time this world has been wounded by the manliness of fools. Oh, let Maurice have his say tonight! And mighty Guy. And my sweet Hilary! Let them have their say. I can only answer them with love.

===Chinese===

害怕我的爱，那在他看来几乎是不洁的；害怕他的父亲，那在他看来几乎是神圣的。英格兰，我的英格兰！他父亲在他身上根深蒂固，哈彭登家族在他身上根深蒂固。他们比十八岁的我更强大，也比对‘哈罗德’的美好回忆更强大。当时我就对内皮尔说，就在那边车灯照亮树干的地方，我以十八岁的口吻对十八岁的他说：‘内皮尔，我觉得我有一具为爱焚烧的身躯。内皮尔，我会用爱去焚烧它，但我除了你之外绝不会对任何男人说“我爱你”，因为那绝不会是真心的。’而我十八岁时说的话，三十岁时的今天依然是真的，除了内皮尔之外我从未对任何男人说过我爱他，因为那都不是真的。我曾献出我自己，带着轻蔑，带着欲望，带着厌恶，带着欢愉，但我始终坚守着我那句愚蠢、孩子般的狂言。我说这些是出于我的耻辱，但如今耻辱只是我脚下的一株野草。我结婚是因为我的身躯饥渴于爱，生来为爱，必须去爱。我原本以为自己会用爱的欢愉毁灭自己的身躯，但我所做的一切只是毁灭了一个好人。赫克托·斯托姆负气去了爱尔兰并丢了性命，就因为一天夜里我在梦呓中低语了内皮尔的名字。或者也许我许多个夜晚都曾这样低语。我告诉他他是在嫉妒一个幽灵，但他不肯相信。如今这一切都已过去。仙女曾为了欲望解开腰带，如今她为了唯一的至爱解开了腰带。人会超越一切，甚至超越欲望：唯有到那时人才能去爱。看哪，‘哈罗德’在微笑，全身银白，在微笑！十二年前莫里斯爵士就是在这里将我牺牲掉的。今晚我必须对他说：‘这就是你干的好事，莫里斯——威尼斯的不幸，你儿子的不幸，以及我十二年的地狱岁月。你满意了吗？’噢上帝啊，这真是一场人间地狱，这整整十二年！如果你曾亲吻过地狱，像我亲吻地狱那样；如果你曾向地狱献祭，像我向地狱献祭自己的身躯那样，你就会明白我的意思。但现在我并不吝惜让莫里斯获得最后的满足，听他告诉我他到底怎么看我。亲爱的，像莫里斯这样的人怎么看待一个人，其实一点都不重要。他们崇拜一切卑鄙可耻之物，他们鄙视一切真正美好之物。从太古之初，这个世界就一直被傻瓜们的男子气概所伤害。噢，今晚就让莫里斯把话说个够吧！还有伟大的盖伊。还有我亲爱的希拉里！让他们说个够吧。我只能用爱来回答他们。

===Original===

How could I answer them but with love? But I can silence them with love! Love, love, love! A glorious word, a matchless word! But isn’t it? Love, love! I am in love. I glory in love, I will die in love! Love, my sweet, love, love! To be in love as I am in love is to be in heaven before hell was made. I am in love!”

The car rushed on, up a wide slope that curved handsomely so that the light played on meadows and startled the beasts of the fields.

“And you are so sure, Iris, that these three men, who have known you all your life and one of whom has loved you with all his heart ever since he saw you walk down South Audley Street, all brown stockings and blue eyes﻿—you are so sure, Iris, that nothing they can say will touch you?”

The lights swept over great lodge-gates standing wide open before a curving avenue of tall trees. We passed beneath them, showering gold on their trunks. The drive shone like a yellow carpet beneath our lights.

“I tell you,” Iris whispered, “I shall be a very Saint George for steadfastness!”

The stork fled up the curving avenue of Sutton Marle. It seemed, to me, to crouch with fear beneath the noble line of trees. They stood above us like towers. I was afraid.

“This is the lion’s den, Iris!”

“Well, I have killed lions, and tigers too, in twelve years’ wandering through hell.”

“But this is the den of the king of lions, Iris! This is the den of the lion of England!”

“Love smiles at lions. Love can never be a clown, but a lion can wear an ass’s skin. Darling, I’m no good at natural history, but I have studied history.”

“You couldn’t mock it so unless you loved it very deeply. You are like a child, dear Iris, daring her father and mother. These trees﻿—”

“But I laugh at the trees of Sutton Marle! I always did, I never could play with them, not even believe in them. I tell you, there is no tree but Harrod’s, my servant, and my master and my playmate tree, Harrod’s. Oh, how Harrod’s hates Sir Maurice! It makes me afraid, to think how Harrod’s hates Maurice Harpenden. Let him beware as he walks beneath it!”

===Chinese===

除了用爱，我还能用什么来回答他们呢？但我可以用爱让他们哑口无言！爱，爱，爱！一个辉煌的字眼，一个无双的字眼！难道不是吗？爱，爱！我恋爱了。我以爱为荣，我愿为爱而死！爱，我亲爱的人，爱，爱！像我这样恋爱，就是在地狱被创造出来之前便置身于天堂。我恋爱了！”

汽车继续疾驰，冲上一道宽阔平缓起伏的斜坡，车灯洒在草甸上，惊动了田野间的牲畜。

“你就这么确信吗，艾丽丝，这三个自幼看着你长大、其中一个自从在南奥德利街看到你穿着棕色长袜、露出一双蓝眼睛款款走过起就全心全意深爱着你的男人——你就这么确信，艾丽丝，无论他们说什么都触动不了你吗？”

车灯掠过巨大的庄园大门，大门敞开着，通向一条由高耸林木夹道的弯曲林荫路。我们从树下穿过，将金光洒在树干上。车道在我们的灯光下宛如一条黄色的地毯。

“我告诉你，”艾丽丝耳语道，“我将像圣乔治一样坚定不移！”

鹳鸟沿着萨顿·马尔弯曲的林荫路飞驰。在我看来，它在这庄严挺拔的树列下仿佛因恐惧而蜷缩。树木宛如高塔耸立在我们上方。我感到害怕。

“这是狮穴啊，艾丽丝！”

“哎，在十二年流浪地狱的岁月里，我杀过狮子，也杀过老虎。”

“可这是万狮之王的巢穴啊，艾丽丝！这是英格兰雄狮的巢穴！”

“爱情在雄狮面前微笑。爱情绝不可能成为小丑，雄狮却可以披上驴皮。亲爱的，我不懂自然历史，但我钻研过历史。”

“除非你深爱着它，否则你不可能如此嘲弄它。你就像个孩子，亲爱的艾丽丝，在挑战自己的父母。这些树木——”

“可我对萨顿·马尔的树木嗤之以鼻！我向来如此，我从来不能跟它们玩耍，甚至不相信它们。我告诉你，世上除了‘哈罗德’没有别的树，‘哈罗德’是我的仆人、我的主人，也是我的玩伴树。噢，‘哈罗德’有多么痛恨莫里斯爵士啊！一想到‘哈罗德’多么痛恨莫里斯·哈彭登，我就感到害怕。他走在树下时可要小心着点！”

===Original===

Then the trees parted from above us and we came into an open place where stood a fountain, and round the fountain we swept a circle and came before the doors of a long white manor house. De Travest’s car stood there. As we drew up beside it the doors of the white house opened and a fat old man stood at the head of the steps. His hair was like his house, quite white.

“Truble, we will go round by the garden,” Iris said.

The fat old butler looked very gravely down at Iris. She was like a small knight at the foot of the broad steps, he a kindly old dragon up above. Oh, he looked so grave!

Iris said to me: “Mr. Truble is my oldest friend. He is a very nice man. Truble, what have you to say to that?”

“Sir Maurice did not expect you, Miss Iris.”

“How, Truble! Sir Maurice knew I was coming!”

“But he did not think you would come, Miss Iris. But his lordship expected you. The gentlemen are in the library.”

“His lordship, Truble, does me too much honour in thinking I can keep my word.﻿ ﻿… Truble, my dear!”

I had been looking round me when that sudden cry shook me like the cry of a bird in pain. The fat old butler was weeping, there was not a doubt of it. There at the head of the broad steps, quite motionless, a broad black shape under his white hair. Iris had him by the shoulder, was shaking him, her hat like a toy against that black shape.

“Truble,” she said, so huskily, “that I should ever have made you cry! My dear, my dear!”

“Sir,” the old man appealed to me down below with a funnily outflung hand. “I never was so ashamed of myself in my life! But it came on me all of a sudden hearing Miss Iris say, here at the doors of Sutton Marle, in a voice as hard as that ash she was always in love with, that about his lordship doing her too much honour about her keeping her word. I held Miss Iris in my arms, sir, when she wasn’t above a year old, and now﻿—I’m sure I beg your pardon, sir. And yours, Miss Iris. I’m sure I don’t know what’s come over me tonight.﻿ ﻿…”

One leather arm had the old man by the shoulder. Iris’s face seemed painted white.

“Truble,” she said, so huskily, “I am so sorry to have upset you. You have been faithful to me, Truble, for thirty years, and now, I suppose, you mustn’t love me any more. You don’t love me any more, Truble?”

“Miss Iris, Miss Iris! There’s no good comes from loving, I see that!”

===Chinese===

随后高处的树木散开，我们来到一片开阔的空地，那里伫立着一座喷泉；我们绕着喷泉画了一个圈，停在了一栋狭长的白色庄园宅邸大门前。德·特拉维斯特的车停在那里。当我们停在它旁边时，白色宅邸的大门打开了，一个发福的老人站在台阶顶端。他的头发和他的宅子一样，一片雪白。

“特鲁布尔，我们从花园那边绕过去，”艾丽丝说。

那个发福的老管家神色极其严肃地低头看着艾丽丝。她就像宽阔台阶脚下的一个矮小骑士，而他是高处一条慈祥的老龙。噢，他看起来是那么严肃！

艾丽丝对我说道：“特鲁布尔先生是我最老的朋友。他是个非常好的人。特鲁布尔，对此你怎么说？”

“莫里斯爵士没料到您会来，艾丽丝小姐。”

“怎么会呢，特鲁布尔！莫里斯爵士明知道我要来的！”

“但他没料到您真的会来，艾丽丝小姐。不过子爵阁下料到您会来的。各位先生们都在书房里。”

“子爵阁下，特鲁布尔，认为我会信守诺言，未免太高抬我了。……特鲁布尔，我亲爱的！”

我正环顾四周，这时一声突如其来的哭喊犹如受伤飞鸟的悲鸣般震颤了我。那个发福的老管家正在哭泣，毫无疑问。他就站在宽阔台阶的顶端，纹丝不动，白发下一副宽厚的黑色身躯。艾丽丝抓着他的肩膀摇晃着他，她的帽子在那个黑色身躯映衬下宛如一个玩具。

“特鲁布尔，”她极其沙哑地说，“我竟然惹你哭了！我亲爱的，我亲爱的！”

“先生，”老人向台阶下方的我求助，滑稽地伸出一只手，“我这辈子从来没这么羞愧过！但我是在萨顿·马尔的大门前，听艾丽丝小姐用像她向来热恋的那棵白蜡树一样冰硬的嗓音，说子爵阁下认为她能信守诺言是太高抬了她，我心里突然一下子受不了了。先生，艾丽丝小姐才刚满一岁的时候，我就把她抱在怀里了，而现在——先生，我真求您原谅。也求您原谅，艾丽丝小姐。我真不知道自己今晚是怎么了。……”

一只皮衣衣袖搂住了老人的肩膀。艾丽丝的面孔仿佛被刷上了一层白漆。

“特鲁布尔，”她极其沙哑地说，“惹你难过我真的很抱歉。特鲁布尔，三十年来你一直对我忠心耿耿，而现在，我想，你不能再爱我了。你不再爱我了，对吗，特鲁布尔？”

“艾丽丝小姐，艾丽丝小姐！爱是换不来好结果的，我看透了！”

===Original===

“Then, Truble, I here and now do release you from giving me a thought ever again. Adieu, Truble.”

Her face painted white, her eyes absorbed in what they did not see, she came down to where I stood. “Come,” she said, expressionless. She could make her words into pieces of iron, and I did not dare to look at the motionless old man at the head of the steps. We skirted the house in silence. I supposed we were on a lawn. The rains of yesterday had not softened the drought, the grass was hard as stone under the feet. I said: “Iris, you are moved already﻿—you who were not to be moved by anything that was said!”

I felt her fingers tightening round my arm. Hers were strong white fingers. “I hadn’t bargained for Truble. He should have been in bed by now. Often in moments of self-hatred and contempt I have taken a little heart from that old man’s devotion. And he would always send me wishes on my birthday. No, I hadn’t bargained for Truble.﻿ ﻿… Look!”

“Why, they are playing bridge!”

“And, dear, how grimly! See, Hilary is looking quite young, he must have a bad hand. And Guy Apollo Belvedere﻿—Oh, he’s thinking!﻿—and then he plays the wrong card! Ah, poor Guy! He always did treat his trumps as though they were tulips, with too much respect. And Sir Maurice! Now, dear heart, what do you think of Sir Maurice? Isn’t he the handsome soldier!”

“Oh, handsome! Napier with a gay sinner’s face.﻿ ﻿…”

“Judge him for me! Oh, do! Here we are, conspirators, whispering. Now, judge me, first, Mr. Townshend of Magralt.”

“Iris, must I! Can I? I can’t!”

“Of course you must, can, will! Speak without thinking. It is only thus that truth is made.”

“He is a good man. His goodness is supported by his principles, his kindness is rebuked by his prejudices. He is not a weak man, but he is the weakest man in that room. He has loved but one woman in his life, and she has crucified his heart on a hundred carnal Calvarys. But he still loves her, and that is why he is the weakest man in that room.”

“And you, satyr, you are the cruellest man I ever met in my life! But judge me, secondly, my lord Viscount de Travest.”

===Chinese===

“那么，特鲁布尔，我现在就正式免除你今后再想起我的义务。别了，特鲁布尔。”

她的面孔白如涂漆，双眼凝视着看不见的虚空，走下台阶来到我站立的地方。“走吧，”她面无表情地说。她能把自己的话变成铁块，而我不敢去看台阶顶端那个纹丝不动的老人。我们在沉默中绕着宅邸走去。我想我们走在一片草坪上。昨天的雨水并没有缓解旱情，草地在脚下硬得像石头一样。我说：“艾丽丝，你已经动容了——你刚才还说无论别人说什么你都不会动容呢！”

我感觉到她的手指紧紧抓住了我的手臂。那是坚强有力的白皙手指。“我没料到特鲁布尔会在。他这会儿本该上床睡了。在自我厌恶与蔑视的时刻，我常常从那个老人的忠诚中汲取一点微薄的安慰。而且每到我生日他总是给我送来祝福。不，我没料到特鲁布尔会在。……看！”

“哎，他们正在打桥牌呢！”

“而且，亲爱的，打得多么冷峻啊！看，希拉里看起来挺年轻的，他手里的牌肯定很烂。还有贝尔维德勒的阿波罗神盖伊——噢，他在思考呢！——然后他出错了牌！啊，可怜的盖伊！他对待将牌向来就像对待郁金香一样，太过于敬重了。还有莫里斯爵士！现在，亲爱的，你觉得莫里斯爵士怎么样？难道不是个英俊的军人吗！”

“噢，英俊！简直就是一个带着寻欢作乐罪人面孔的内皮尔。……”

“替我评价一下他！噢，快评评！瞧我们俩，像阴谋家一样在窃窃私语。现在，首先替我评评马格拉尔特的汤森先生。”

“艾丽丝，非得这样吗！我行吗？我做不到！”

“你当然必须、能够、而且一定会做到的！不假思索地说出来。只有这样才能道出真理。”

“他是个好人。他的善良由他的原则支撑，他的体贴受到他的偏见的谴责。他不是个软弱的人，但他却是那间屋子里最软弱的人。他这辈子只爱过一个女人，而那个女人却在一百个肉欲的受难苦路上将他的心钉在了十字架上。但他依然深爱着她，这就是为什么他是那间屋子里最软弱的人。”

“而你，萨堤尔，你是我这辈子见过的最残忍的男人！那么其次，替我评评德·特拉维斯特子爵阁下吧。”

===Original===

“He is the elder brother of honour. He is that rarest of men, a schoolboy who has grown out of his schooldays but remains, by strength of will, a schoolboy. He prefers to be that. He never did an unworthy thing, and has thought less mean ones than most people. Like all decent Englishmen, he is like a woman: he knows everything without ever having been taught anything.
